# 7c-3 Mesh Tools Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Give the mesh the tools the spec's §3.6 names: Decimate (quadric error, manifold-preserving, within a tolerance), Check Thin Walls and Check Trapped Volumes (with
highlights and the spec's warnings), Drain Holes (at points and automatic, on the implicit), Mesh to Solid (an OCCT shim, refused above 100,000 triangles, STEP-exportable);
3MF with our own `ZipWriter` and File ▸ Export 3MF…; the export streamed to the file window by window (Errata D12); the Cut view; `ImplicitBench` rows i-5 and i-6; and the
spec §7 lightweight-bracket acceptance test.

**Architecture:** Every algorithm is a pure function in `CreatorImplicit` on arrays or on the existing `TriangleMesh`/`ImplicitSolid`, portable to Linux and Windows
(Errata D11). The nodes in `CreatorNodes` are thin. Two things are new in kind. A **`MeshStream`** is a second `MeshPayload`: while an export streams
(`MeshQuality.streamedExport`) a Mesh node hands over the implicit and its resolution instead of a mesh, Decimate adds a stage to it, the checks look at a copy of it,
and `StreamedExport` meshes it window by window into a `MeshSink` (`STLFileWriter`, `ThreeMFWriter`), so the file is never in memory. The **Cut view** is a plane in the
viewport model and a clip in the fragment shaders, reusing 7b's slice pose and handle. Only the OCCT shim (`occt_mesh_to_solid`) and the Metal shader changes are macOS-only.

**Tech Stack:** Swift 6.4 (strict concurrency), Swift Testing, SwiftPM, OpenCascade 7.9 through the C++ shim, runtime-compiled Metal Shading Language, Apple's Compression
framework behind `#if canImport(Compression)`. No third-party libraries, no new target, no `Package.swift` change.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-13-implicit-lattices-design.md` §3.6, §4.5, §4.6, §4.7, §5.1, §5.3, §6 (rows i-4 to i-6), §7 and §8 (the 7c-3 row), and its **Errata
D11** (portability), **D12** (the streamed export) and **D15** (GPU meshing details), under the parent spec
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-metalcreator-vertical-slice-design.md` with all its Errata. Repo: worktree `MetalCreator-7c-3`, branch `7c-3`.

**Base:** the real 7c-core, complete, at `1ad1415` in `/Users/maxburger/Developer/MetalCreator-7c-core`: **4,251 tests on macOS with 12 "Test run with" lines, green on Linux and
Windows CI with 11.** The branch `7c-3` is at `1ad1415`. **First run `git merge master`** in the worktree: master is `1ad1415` plus xp-adopt-s1b (`a8061a4`, MetalUI S1b), which already
removes the XP-G11 known-issue guard from `SDLChromeWindowTests` (7c-2 and 7c-1g carry the same change), so the chrome tests pass on macOS from the start and **this plan does not edit the
guard** (checked: on `1ad1415` itself both guarded tests fail with "Known issue was not recorded", which is why the merge comes first). Then execute this plan. Every name below is
7c-core's real one: `TriangleMesh`, `MeshTopology`, `ImplicitMesher`, `BrickPlan`, `STLWriter`, `ExportRun`, `ExportPlan`, `OutputNode`, `MeshInput`, `MeshNode`. Test counts are "base + N",
**base being what the merged tree counts** (4,251 on `1ad1415`; master's own additions are not this plan's): Tasks 1 to 11 add **168** (the full suite in the scratch copy on which this plan was
verified counted 4,419 in its 12 "Test run with" lines) and Task 12's acceptance test adds the 169th once 7c-2 has merged. **Per task** (not cumulative): +9, +7, +13, +26, +11, +17, +22, +9, +26, +22, +6
(the three benchmarks count: they are skipped in a plain run). Twenty of the 169 are macOS-only (the OCCT ones, `ViewportClipRenderTests`, `i-4z`, `i-6`, the acceptance test), so Linux and Windows gain
about **149**; CI is the check. The library count is 77 after 7c-core and
this track adds **5 nodes** (Drain Holes, Decimate, Check Thin Walls, Check Trapped Volumes, Mesh to Solid): 82; with 7c-2's three lattices, 85.

## Errata and decisions this plan applies

| | What the plan does |
|---|---|
| **D11** portable | Everything new in `CreatorImplicit` and `CreatorNodes` builds on Linux and Windows. The one Apple framework, `Compression`, is imported by two files, each inside `#if canImport(Compression)`, and `ImplicitPortabilityTests` learns the rule (Task 7); elsewhere zip entries are **stored**. OCCT is macOS-only, so `occt_mesh_to_solid`, its wrapper and its tests live in macOS-only targets (the node and the `Kernel` requirement are portable and refuse on a kernel without a B-rep). The Cut view's *state* is portable and tested everywhere; its shaders and controls are Metal-only (`CutViewModel.isAvailable`). |
| **D12** streamed export | Tasks 7 to 9. Bricks are meshed in a fixed order, a window of whole brick columns at a time; each window goes straight to the STL or 3MF writer; vertices on the plane two windows share are matched by their bits and written once. Decimate runs per window, pinning the shared plane. The checks run on a copy (below). i-4 keeps its targets (under 60 s, under 4 GB): 7c-core measured 4.53 GB held in memory for the same export, and the streamed export's number is in `docs/verification/performance.md`. **A 3MF of D12's own case (about 150 million triangles) has some 10 GB of XML in one zip entry, so the zip is Zip64 where a field overflows** (Task 7; decision 5): without it the one export D12 exists for would work for STL only. |
| **D15** GPU meshing | Not built here: the CPU mesher stays the reference, and `StreamingMesher` calls `MeshBuilder` and `ImplicitMesher.snap` as they are. See "Files shared with other tracks" for what 7c-1g's side must keep. |

### Decisions the plan makes where the spec is silent

The ones a person might disagree with are under **User decisions** in the hand-off.

1. **Checks in a streamed export run on an in-memory copy, not per brick.** D12 allows either. A thin-wall ray or a flood fill needs the neighbouring bricks, so a per-window check
   would miss walls across a window's plane; a copy is global and exact whenever the part fits in 2,000,000 triangles at the export resolution, and otherwise as much coarser as brings it
   to that size, which the warning says ("approximate: coarser than the export"). Decimate is exact per window (its edge is pinned).
2. **Tool previews are not refined.** A tool's output has a new ID and no `source`, so the progressive preview never replaces it by a refinement of the unprocessed implicit;
   it shows the Mesh node's first pass (or its fixed preview resolution) through the tool. Checks on a preview say "(approximate: preview resolution)".
3. **Wording**: one place and one void are singular ("1 place", "1 enclosed void can't drain."); the label goes before the full stop.
4. **Drain Holes defaults to Automatic**; a hole is **one-sided**: it runs from its point along the direction until it leaves the part (not through the whole part, which the spec
   does not ask for); the lowest point of a void is the cell centre farthest along the direction (the one nearest the middle of the lowest layer). A hole narrower than four of the check's cells
   (2 mm for a part up to 150 mm across) warns that Check Trapped Volumes may still report the void.
5. **3MF is Zip64 where it needs to be, deflated on a Mac and stored elsewhere** (Task 7). A size, an offset or an entry count that reaches 4 GB is written in 64 bits and the file says so;
   below that the zip is the plain one every reader knows. Without this the 3MF of D12's 150-million-triangle case (about 10 GB of XML) would be refused on every platform, because the limit
   applies to the *uncompressed* size as well and deflate does not help. Unit tests lower the threshold to a hundred bytes (`ZipWriterTests`); bench `i-4z` writes a 4.7 GB entry and the system's
   `unzip -t` reads it. The writer keeps the XML in two temporary files until `finish()`, so an export needs about twice its XML size free on disk (the temporary folder, then the destination).
6. **The Cut view's plane is one of the three axis planes** (the one nearest the view when it is switched on), movable along its normal by the slice handle, with a Flip.
7. **Mesh to Solid on a stream** meshes it into memory with its Decimate stages applied; the 100,000 cap is on that result.
8. **The check's cell is 0.5 mm, not the spec's 2 mm, and is made coarser only to stay within 32 million cells** (Task 4). A 2 mm cell cannot see through the default 2 mm drain hole, so the check
   that follows Drain Holes would keep reporting the void. 0.5 mm holds up to a 150 mm cube (a plate a metre square and 10 mm thick gets about 0.75 mm); a 300 mm cube gets 1 mm cells and a
   metre cube 3 mm, where a 2 mm hole is sub-cell again. That case is **documented and said, not solved**: the check adds a "Grid cell" fact and a warning when its cell is above 0.5 mm, and Drain Holes
   warns when its diameter is below four cells (`CheckWording.coarseGrid`, `smallHole`). Recorded as a spec erratum (Task 12).
9. **File ▸ Export 3MF… is the one way in** (no shortcut: ⌘E and ⇧⌘E are taken); the top bar's Export menu stays STEP and STL. The spec names only the File menu.
10. **A check of a streamed export runs on a copy of at most 2,000,000 triangles**; a larger part is checked coarser and the warning says "(approximate: coarser than the export)" (decision 1).
11. **A blank object name is "Part"** in a 3MF (a slicer lists an empty name badly).
12. **The Mesh to Solid shim shares vertices and edges by index and does not call `BRepBuilderAPI_Sewing`** (Task 6; 26 s against about 13 s on 100,000 triangles). After Decimate the neighbouring planes
    differ by float noise, so fewer faces merge than on an exact mesh; the solid is still valid and has the mesh's volume (`aDecimatedMeshBecomesAValidSolidOf…`).

## Global Constraints

Every task's requirements include these (7c-core's, with this plan's counts).

- Swift 6 strict concurrency (`swift-tools-version: 6.4`, `swiftLanguageModes: [.v6]`). No `@unchecked Sendable` or `nonisolated(unsafe)`; shared mutable state is an actor, `Mutex` or `@MainActor`.
  Untyped `throws` closures where `OCCTKernel` calls the shim under `serialized` (a Swift 6.4 IRGen crash with typed throws there).
- Tests use Swift Testing (`import Testing`, `@Test`, `#expect`), never XCTest. Models are `@MainActor @Observable`; behaviour lives in models and views are thin; one type per file (an
  extension, or a small helper beside it, is fine); no force unwraps, no `try!`; no `DispatchQueue`; `Task.sleep(for:)`; `FormatStyle`, never `String(format:)` or a `Formatter`
  subclass (the 3MF's hand-written number text is not display formatting, and a test holds it to `FormatStyle`'s).
- `swiftlint lint --strict` reports zero violations (lines to 140 columns, trailing commas in multi-line collection literals, function bodies to 50 lines, types to 250).
- **A full `swift test` passes only if** the exit code is 0, no line says "recorded an issue" or "failed after" (Swift Testing uses glyphs, not ✘), and **12 "Test run with" lines**
  appear on macOS (11 on Linux and Windows): this plan adds no test target.
- **`CreatorImplicit` is portable (Errata D11)**: nothing in it imports Metal, simd, CoreGraphics or another Apple-only framework except files named `Metal*.swift`, and `Compression`
  only under `#if canImport(Compression)`; no `MTL…` type is named outside them. `ImplicitPortabilityTests` enforces it.
- OCCT stays behind `Kernel`; all OCCT work runs under `OCCTKernel.serialized`; no C++ exception crosses into Swift; every shim message is user-facing and unprefixed.
- Strings the spec gives, used exactly: node names `Decimate`, `Check Thin Walls`, `Check Trapped Volumes`, `Drain Holes`, `Mesh to Solid`; settings `tolerance`, `minimum`, `diameter`,
  `direction`, `mode` (At Points, Automatic), `points`; "Walls thinner than 0.4 mm in N places (thinnest 0.21 mm).", "N enclosed voids can't drain.", "(approximate: preview resolution)",
  "Mesh has N triangles; Mesh to Solid takes at most 100,000. Decimate it first or coarsen the resolution.", "Output 'Insole' is a mesh; STEP needs solids. Export STL or 3MF, or convert it
  with Mesh to Solid.", the menu "File > Export 3MF…".
- MetalUI gaps are logged in `docs/metalui-gaps.md` (entry and summary row) and never worked around; this plan found none. Never modify `../MetalUI`, never edit the MetalUI path in `Package.swift`,
  never take screenshots.
- Never `pkill` or `killall` by a broad pattern (`swift-test`, `swift test`, `swift-build`, `xctest`): other sessions run `swift test` on this Mac. Kill only your own processes, by PID or by a
  pattern containing your own worktree path.
- Run every command from the worktree. Commit messages end with the session's attribution lines.
- Verification per task is light: `swift build --build-tests` (no warning beyond the baseline's `ContextMenuTests.swift` one and linker `ld: warning` lines), the touched suites with `--filter`,
  and `swiftlint lint --strict`. One full `swift test` runs after the last task.

## Review Focus

What the spec implies and a person will meet, but no happy-path test shows; most likely first. Each line has its test, named, in the task that owns the code.

1. **A crack where two windows of the streamed export meet.** The shared plane's vertices are made twice and matched by their bits; a miss writes a vertex twice, which an STL hides and a
   slicer's repair would not. Expected: the streamed mesh is the very triangles of the whole mesher's, whatever the window size, and a 3MF read back is one closed manifold, with Decimate too.
   Tests: Task 8 `aSphereInManyWindowsIsTheMeshTheWholeMesherMakes`, `theWindowSizeChangesNothingInTheOutput`, `decimatingEachWindowKeepsTheWholeClosedAndTheShapeWithinTheTolerance`,
   `aStreamToAFileHoldsTheSameTrianglesAsTheMesh`; Task 9 `aMeshOutputIsA3MFObjectNamedAfterItWithAClosedSurfaceAtExportResolution`.
2. **A cancelled or failed export leaves a half file.** Expected: nothing at the destination and no `.partial` or `mc-3mf-` temporary. Tests: Task 7 `aCancelledZipLeavesNoFile`,
   `aCancelledExportLeavesNoFileAndNoTemporaries`, `aMisusedWriterThrowsInsteadOfCrashing`; Task 9 `aCancelledExportLeavesNothingInTheFolder` (the folder is listed), `cancelStopsTheExportAndLeavesNoFile`.
3. **Check Thin Walls crying wolf, or missing a wall.** A plain box meshed by the mesher must have no thin place at its sharp edges; a 0.2 mm wall must be one place, not two shells' worth, and a
   0.5 mm wall none. Tests: Task 3 `aMeshOfABoxFromTheMesherHasNoFalsePlacesAtItsEdges`, `aSharpCornerIsNotAWall`, `aWallOfAFifthOfAMillimetreIsFoundWhereverTheCubeHasOne`, `aWallThickerThanTheMinimumIsLeftAlone`,
   `twoThinPlatesApartAreTwoPlaces`.
4. **A void missed because its wall is thinner than a grid cell, or a void reported that is smaller than the grid can see.** Expected: a 0.2 mm wall still seals a 0.31 mm grid; a 0.2 mm cavity is not a
   void; an open mesh says it has no inside. Tests: Task 4 `aVoidInsideAThinWallIsStillSealed`, `aVoidTooSmallForTheGridIsNotFound`, `aMeshThatIsNotClosedHasNoInside`.
5. **Mesh to Solid on a mesh OCCT would choke on.** Open, inside-out, over the cap, a sliver of two vertices 1e-8 apart. Expected: OCCT's reason or the spec's cap message, never a crash or a
   solid that fails `BRepCheck`. Tests: Task 6 `aMeshThatIsNotClosedSaysSo`, `aMeshWoundInsideOutSaysSo`, `tooFewTrianglesAndBadIndicesAreRefused`, `aSliverCollapsedByTheWeldTakesItsTriangleAndKeepsTheMeshClosed`,
   `aMeshAboveTheLimitIsRefusedInTheSpecsWords`, `aDecimatedMeshBecomesAValidSolidOf…`; Task 9 `meshToSolidTakesAStreamWithItsDecimationApplied`.
6. **A 3MF past 4 GB.** Zip64 where a size, an offset or the count reaches 4 GB, and plain zip below. Expected: the entries read back with sizes above 32 bits, and the system's `unzip -t` accepts a 4.7 GB entry.
   Tests: Task 7 `anEntryAndAnOffsetPastTheZip64LimitAreWrittenInFullAndReadBack`, `aSmallFileIsPlainZipWithNoZip64Records`, `aZip64FileHasItsEndRecordsAndAnExtraFieldForEachOverflow`,
   `manyEntriesPastSixtyFiveThousandAreCountedInZip64`; Task 11 `aZipEntryPastFourGigabytesIsValidZip64` (macOS, release).
7. (Also pinned) a 3MF a slicer reads: names escaped and never blank, six digits, the OPC parts, deterministic bytes (Task 7 `namesAreMadeSafeForXML`, `numbersHaveSixSignificantDigitsLikeFormatStyleWritesThem`,
   `aFileReadsBackAsAnOPCPackageWithNamedObjectsInMillimetres`, `theSameInputGivesTheSameBytes`); and a cut that picks what is left (Task 10 `thePickSeesOnlyWhatIsLeft`).

## Tasks



### Task 1: Quadric error decimation (`MeshDecimator`)

The algorithm of spec §4.5 as a pure function on arrays, so the Decimate node (Task 2) and the streamed export (Task 8) share it. It is
written for speed (struct-of-arrays state, a binary heap with lazy deletion by per-vertex stamps) because row i-5 asks for 2,097,152
triangles in under 10 s.

How a collapse is judged (the spec's three refusals, plus one):

1. **Manifold**: the link condition. The two ends of the edge must share exactly as many neighbours as the edge has faces (two for an
   interior edge), or the collapse would pinch the surface into a non-manifold vertex or edge. A closed mesh stops at four triangles.
2. **No flipped face**: every triangle around a moving end, except the two that vanish, keeps its normal within about 78 degrees of
   what it was and keeps an area.
3. **Within the tolerance**: the cost of a collapse is the quadric error at the new vertex, the sum of squared distances to the *unit*
   planes of every triangle the vertex has absorbed. Its square root is at least the distance to the farthest such plane, so a collapse
   whose cost is within tolerance² leaves the new vertex within the tolerance of every plane it replaced. This is a rigorous but
   conservative bound (a flat patch with noise of ε costs √k·ε for k absorbed triangles); the tests measure the true sampled Hausdorff
   distance both ways and hold it to the tolerance.
4. **Open edges are pinned**: a vertex on an edge with one face never moves and never goes. A closed mesh has none. A window of the
   streamed export (Task 8) is open exactly where it meets its neighbour, so the shared plane comes out as it went in and the pieces
   still fit.

An edge with more than two faces makes the mesh non-manifold: decimation refuses (`MeshDecimator.Failure.nonManifold`).

**Files:**
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/SIMD3Double+Vector.swift`, `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Decimation/Quadric.swift`,
  `EdgeHeap.swift`, `MeshDecimationState.swift`, `MeshDecimationState+Collapse.swift`, `MeshDecimator.swift`
- Test: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/MeshDecimatorTests.swift`; test support `Support/MeshFixtures+Shapes.swift` (a subdivided cube, a flat
  patch, a sphere as a `TriangleMesh`) and `Support/MeshDistance.swift` (brute-force closest point and sampled Hausdorff distance)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `TriangleMesh`, `MeshTopology`, `MeshFixtures` (7c-core).
- Produces: `MeshDecimator.decimate(positions: [SIMD3<Float>], triangles: [UInt32], tolerance: Double) throws -> MeshDecimator.Output`
  (`positions`, `triangles`, `origin: [Int32]` mapping each output vertex to the input vertex it stands for, `collapses`);
  `MeshDecimator.Failure.nonManifold(edges:)` with `userMessage`; `SIMD3<Double>.cross/dot/length` (internal).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/Support/MeshFixtures+Shapes.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorImplicit

extension MeshFixtures {
    /// A cube from (0, 0, 0) to (1, 1, 1) with `divisions` squares along each edge of each face, two triangles to a square, wound
    /// counter-clockwise seen from outside, with vertices shared between faces.
    static func subdividedCube(divisions n: Int) -> TriangleMesh {
        var positions: [SIMD3<Float>] = []
        var numbers: [[Int]: UInt32] = [:]
        var triangles: [UInt32] = []
        func vertex(_ p: Vector3) -> UInt32 {
            let key = [Int((p.x * Double(n)).rounded()), Int((p.y * Double(n)).rounded()), Int((p.z * Double(n)).rounded())]
            if let known = numbers[key] { return known }
            positions.append(SIMD3(Float(p.x), Float(p.y), Float(p.z)))
            numbers[key] = UInt32(positions.count - 1)
            return UInt32(positions.count - 1)
        }
        // Each face: its origin and the two directions u and v, with u × v pointing out of the cube.
        let faces: [(origin: Vector3, u: Vector3, v: Vector3)] = [
            (Vector3(1, 0, 0), .unitY, .unitZ), (Vector3(0, 0, 0), .unitZ, .unitY),
            (Vector3(0, 1, 0), .unitZ, .unitX), (Vector3(0, 0, 0), .unitX, .unitZ),
            (Vector3(0, 0, 1), .unitX, .unitY), (Vector3(0, 0, 0), .unitY, .unitX),
        ]
        for face in faces {
            for i in 0..<n {
                for j in 0..<n {
                    func corner(_ di: Int, _ dj: Int) -> UInt32 {
                        vertex(face.origin + face.u * (Double(i + di) / Double(n)) + face.v * (Double(j + dj) / Double(n)))
                    }
                    let (p00, p10, p11, p01) = (corner(0, 0), corner(1, 0), corner(1, 1), corner(0, 1))
                    triangles += [p00, p10, p11, p00, p11, p01]
                }
            }
        }
        return TriangleMesh(positions: positions, triangles: triangles)
    }

    /// A flat square patch in the XY plane, `divisions` squares along each side, 10 mm wide, facing +Z. It is open: its border has
    /// edges with one face.
    static func patch(divisions n: Int) -> TriangleMesh {
        var positions: [SIMD3<Float>] = []
        for j in 0...n {
            for i in 0...n { positions.append(SIMD3(Float(i) * 10 / Float(n), Float(j) * 10 / Float(n), 0)) }
        }
        var triangles: [UInt32] = []
        for j in 0..<n {
            for i in 0..<n {
                let (a, b) = (UInt32(j * (n + 1) + i), UInt32(j * (n + 1) + i + 1))
                let (c, d) = (a + UInt32(n + 1), b + UInt32(n + 1))
                triangles += [a, b, d, a, d, c]
            }
        }
        return TriangleMesh(positions: positions, triangles: triangles)
    }

    /// A sphere of `radius` as a `TriangleMesh`.
    static func sphereMesh(radius: Double, subdivisions: Int) -> TriangleMesh {
        let shape = sphere(radius: radius, subdivisions: subdivisions)
        return TriangleMesh(positions: shape.positions.map { SIMD3(Float($0.x), Float($0.y), Float($0.z)) }, triangles: shape.indices)
    }
}
```

Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/Support/MeshDistance.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorImplicit

/// Brute-force distances between meshes, for the tests of the tools that change a surface.
enum MeshDistance {
    /// The point of the triangle (a, b, c) nearest to p (Ericson, Real-Time Collision Detection 5.1.5).
    static func closest(_ p: Vector3, _ a: Vector3, _ b: Vector3, _ c: Vector3) -> Vector3 {
        let (ab, ac, ap) = (b - a, c - a, p - a)
        let (d1, d2) = (ab.dot(ap), ac.dot(ap))
        if d1 <= 0, d2 <= 0 { return a }
        let bp = p - b
        let (d3, d4) = (ab.dot(bp), ac.dot(bp))
        if d3 >= 0, d4 <= d3 { return b }
        let vc = d1 * d4 - d3 * d2
        if vc <= 0, d1 >= 0, d3 <= 0 { return a + ab * (d1 / (d1 - d3)) }
        let cp = p - c
        let (d5, d6) = (ab.dot(cp), ac.dot(cp))
        if d6 >= 0, d5 <= d6 { return c }
        let vb = d5 * d2 - d1 * d6
        if vb <= 0, d2 >= 0, d6 <= 0 { return a + ac * (d2 / (d2 - d6)) }
        let va = d3 * d6 - d5 * d4
        if va <= 0, d4 - d3 >= 0, d5 - d6 >= 0 { return b + (c - b) * ((d4 - d3) / ((d4 - d3) + (d5 - d6))) }
        let denominator = 1 / (va + vb + vc)
        return a + ab * (vb * denominator) + ac * (vc * denominator)
    }

    /// The distance from `p` to the surface of `mesh`.
    static func distance(from p: Vector3, to mesh: TriangleMesh) -> Double {
        var best = Double.infinity
        for index in 0..<mesh.triangleCount {
            let (a, b, c) = mesh.corners(of: index)
            best = min(best, (closest(p, a, b, c) - p).length)
        }
        return best
    }

    /// The largest distance from a vertex or a triangle's centre of `a` to the surface of `b`.
    static func oneWay(from a: TriangleMesh, to b: TriangleMesh) -> Double {
        var worst = 0.0
        for vertex in 0..<a.vertexCount { worst = max(worst, distance(from: a.point(vertex), to: b)) }
        for index in 0..<a.triangleCount {
            let (p, q, r) = a.corners(of: index)
            worst = max(worst, distance(from: (p + q + r) * (1.0 / 3), to: b))
        }
        return worst
    }

    /// The sampled Hausdorff distance: the larger of the two one-way distances.
    static func hausdorff(_ a: TriangleMesh, _ b: TriangleMesh) -> Double {
        max(oneWay(from: a, to: b), oneWay(from: b, to: a))
    }
}
```

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

/// Decimation (implicit spec §4.5, §7): fewer triangles, the surface within the tolerance, still a closed manifold.
struct MeshDecimatorTests {
    func decimated(_ mesh: TriangleMesh, tolerance: Double) throws -> TriangleMesh {
        let output = try MeshDecimator.decimate(positions: mesh.positions, triangles: mesh.triangles, tolerance: tolerance)
        return TriangleMesh(positions: output.positions, triangles: output.triangles)
    }

    @Test func aSubdividedCubeCollapsesToItsCornersAndFaces() throws {
        let cube = MeshFixtures.subdividedCube(divisions: 6)
        #expect(cube.triangleCount == 432 && MeshTopology(cube).isClosedManifold)
        let result = try decimated(cube, tolerance: 0.001)
        let topology = MeshTopology(result)
        #expect(topology.isClosedManifold && topology.eulerCharacteristic == 2)
        #expect(result.triangleCount <= 24, "a cube needs 12 triangles; got \(result.triangleCount)")
        #expect(abs(result.signedVolume - 1) < 1e-9 && abs(result.surfaceArea - 6) < 1e-9)
        #expect(MeshDistance.hausdorff(cube, result) < 1e-6)
        let corners = Set(result.positions.filter { $0.x.rounded() == $0.x && $0.y.rounded() == $0.y && $0.z.rounded() == $0.z })
        #expect(corners.count == 8, "all eight corners survive")
    }

    @Test func aSphereLosesTrianglesAndStaysWithinTheTolerance() throws {
        let sphere = MeshFixtures.sphereMesh(radius: 10, subdivisions: 3)
        let tolerance = 0.3
        let result = try decimated(sphere, tolerance: tolerance)
        let topology = MeshTopology(result)
        #expect(topology.isClosedManifold && topology.eulerCharacteristic == 2)
        #expect(result.triangleCount < sphere.triangleCount * 9 / 10, "\(sphere.triangleCount) → \(result.triangleCount)")
        let distance = MeshDistance.hausdorff(sphere, result)
        #expect(distance <= tolerance, "Hausdorff \(distance) against a tolerance of \(tolerance)")
        #expect(result.signedVolume > 0)
    }

    @Test func aLargerToleranceKeepsFewerTriangles() throws {
        let sphere = MeshFixtures.sphereMesh(radius: 10, subdivisions: 3)
        let tight = try decimated(sphere, tolerance: 0.3)
        let loose = try decimated(sphere, tolerance: 1)
        #expect(loose.triangleCount < tight.triangleCount && tight.triangleCount < sphere.triangleCount)
        #expect(MeshDistance.hausdorff(sphere, loose) <= 1 && MeshDistance.hausdorff(sphere, tight) <= 0.3)
    }

    @Test func aToleranceOfZeroLeavesACurvedSurfaceAlone() throws {
        let sphere = MeshFixtures.sphereMesh(radius: 10, subdivisions: 3)
        let result = try decimated(sphere, tolerance: 0)
        #expect(result.triangleCount == sphere.triangleCount)
    }

    @Test func aTetrahedronIsTheSmallestMeshLeft() throws {
        let result = try decimated(MeshFixtures.tetrahedron, tolerance: 100)
        #expect(result.triangleCount == 4 && MeshTopology(result).isClosedManifold)
        let blob = try decimated(MeshFixtures.sphereMesh(radius: 1, subdivisions: 3), tolerance: 100)
        #expect(blob.triangleCount >= 4 && MeshTopology(blob).isClosedManifold)
    }

    @Test func anOpenPatchKeepsItsEdgeExactly() throws {
        let patch = MeshFixtures.patch(divisions: 10)
        let output = try MeshDecimator.decimate(positions: patch.positions, triangles: patch.triangles, tolerance: 0.001)
        #expect(output.triangles.count < patch.triangles.count / 3, "the flat inside collapses")
        let border = { (p: SIMD3<Float>) in p.x == 0 || p.x == 10 || p.y == 0 || p.y == 10 }
        let before = Set(patch.positions.filter(border))
        let after = Set(output.positions.filter(border))
        #expect(before.count == 40 && before == after, "every border vertex is where it was")
        let result = TriangleMesh(positions: output.positions, triangles: output.triangles)
        let topology = MeshTopology(result)
        #expect(topology.overfullEdgeCount == 0 && topology.inconsistentEdgeCount == 0 && topology.degenerateTriangleCount == 0)
        #expect(topology.openEdgeCount == 40, "the border has the same 40 edges")
        #expect(abs(result.surfaceArea - 100) < 1e-4)
    }

    @Test func eachOutputVertexNamesTheInputVertexItCameFrom() throws {
        let patch = MeshFixtures.patch(divisions: 6)
        let output = try MeshDecimator.decimate(positions: patch.positions, triangles: patch.triangles, tolerance: 0.001)
        #expect(output.origin.count == output.positions.count)
        let border = { (p: SIMD3<Float>) in p.x == 0 || p.x == 10 || p.y == 0 || p.y == 10 }
        for (index, origin) in output.origin.enumerated() where border(output.positions[index]) {
            #expect(patch.positions[Int(origin)] == output.positions[index])
        }
    }

    @Test func threeFacesOnOneEdgeCannotBeSimplified() {
        let positions: [SIMD3<Float>] = [SIMD3(0, 0, 0), SIMD3(1, 0, 0), SIMD3(0, 1, 0), SIMD3(0, -1, 0), SIMD3(0, 0, 1)]
        let fin: [UInt32] = [0, 1, 2, 0, 3, 1, 0, 1, 4]
        #expect(throws: MeshDecimator.Failure.nonManifold(edges: 1)) {
            try MeshDecimator.decimate(positions: positions, triangles: fin, tolerance: 1)
        }
        #expect(MeshDecimator.Failure.nonManifold(edges: 1).userMessage.contains("more than two faces"))
    }

    @Test func triangleListsWithBadIndicesAreIgnoredNotTrusted() throws {
        let output = try MeshDecimator.decimate(positions: MeshFixtures.tetrahedron.positions,
                                                triangles: MeshFixtures.tetrahedron.triangles + [0, 0, 1, 0, 1, 99, 7], tolerance: 0)
        #expect(output.triangles.count == 12)
    }
}
```

```swift
/// The vector maths the mesh tools share, on `SIMD3<Double>`: the standard library has no cross or dot product, and the `simd`
/// module that has them is Apple's (implicit spec Errata D11).
extension SIMD3 where Scalar == Double {
    func cross(_ other: SIMD3<Double>) -> SIMD3<Double> {
        SIMD3(y * other.z - z * other.y, z * other.x - x * other.z, x * other.y - y * other.x)
    }

    func dot(_ other: SIMD3<Double>) -> Double { (self * other).sum() }

    var length: Double { dot(self).squareRoot() }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Decimation/Quadric.swift`:

```swift
/// A quadric error metric (Garland and Heckbert; implicit spec §4.5): the sum, over some planes, of the squared distance to each
/// plane, kept as the ten distinct entries of a symmetric 4 × 4 matrix. `error(at:)` is that sum at a point. The planes are unit
/// planes with equal weight, so √error is at least the distance to the farthest plane: a collapse whose √error is within the
/// tolerance leaves its new vertex within the tolerance of every triangle plane it has absorbed.
struct Quadric: Sendable {
    var a2 = 0.0, ab = 0.0, ac = 0.0, ad = 0.0
    var b2 = 0.0, bc = 0.0, bd = 0.0
    var c2 = 0.0, cd = 0.0
    var d2 = 0.0

    init() {}

    /// The quadric of the plane through `point` with the unit `normal`.
    init(normal n: SIMD3<Double>, through point: SIMD3<Double>) {
        let d = -(n * point).sum()
        a2 = n.x * n.x
        ab = n.x * n.y
        ac = n.x * n.z
        ad = n.x * d
        b2 = n.y * n.y
        bc = n.y * n.z
        bd = n.y * d
        c2 = n.z * n.z
        cd = n.z * d
        d2 = d * d
    }

    static func + (lhs: Quadric, rhs: Quadric) -> Quadric {
        var sum = Quadric()
        sum.a2 = lhs.a2 + rhs.a2
        sum.ab = lhs.ab + rhs.ab
        sum.ac = lhs.ac + rhs.ac
        sum.ad = lhs.ad + rhs.ad
        sum.b2 = lhs.b2 + rhs.b2
        sum.bc = lhs.bc + rhs.bc
        sum.bd = lhs.bd + rhs.bd
        sum.c2 = lhs.c2 + rhs.c2
        sum.cd = lhs.cd + rhs.cd
        sum.d2 = lhs.d2 + rhs.d2
        return sum
    }

    static func += (lhs: inout Quadric, rhs: Quadric) {
        lhs = lhs + rhs
    }

    /// The sum of the squared plane distances at `p` (never below 0).
    func error(at p: SIMD3<Double>) -> Double {
        let value = a2 * p.x * p.x + 2 * ab * p.x * p.y + 2 * ac * p.x * p.z + 2 * ad * p.x
            + b2 * p.y * p.y + 2 * bc * p.y * p.z + 2 * bd * p.y
            + c2 * p.z * p.z + 2 * cd * p.z + d2
        return max(value, 0)
    }

    /// The point where the error is least, or `nil` when the planes do not fix one (all parallel, or nearly so).
    func optimum() -> SIMD3<Double>? {
        let det = a2 * (b2 * c2 - bc * bc) - ab * (ab * c2 - bc * ac) + ac * (ab * bc - b2 * ac)
        let scale = (a2 + b2 + c2) * (a2 + b2 + c2) * (a2 + b2 + c2)
        guard scale > 0, abs(det) > 1e-9 * scale else { return nil }
        let (x, y, z) = (-ad, -bd, -cd)
        let dx = x * (b2 * c2 - bc * bc) - ab * (y * c2 - bc * z) + ac * (y * bc - b2 * z)
        let dy = a2 * (y * c2 - bc * z) - x * (ab * c2 - bc * ac) + ac * (ab * z - y * ac)
        let dz = a2 * (b2 * z - y * bc) - ab * (ab * z - y * ac) + x * (ab * bc - b2 * ac)
        return SIMD3(dx / det, dy / det, dz / det)
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Decimation/EdgeHeap.swift`:

```swift
/// A binary min-heap of edge collapses by cost. Entries are never updated in place: a vertex's `stamp` counts the changes to it, and an
/// entry whose stamps are no longer the vertices' is stale and dropped when it comes to the top.
struct EdgeHeap: Sendable {
    struct Entry: Sendable {
        var cost: Double
        var u: Int32
        var v: Int32
        var stampU: UInt32
        var stampV: UInt32
    }

    private var items: [Entry] = []

    var isEmpty: Bool { items.isEmpty }

    mutating func reserve(_ count: Int) {
        items.reserveCapacity(count)
    }

    mutating func push(_ entry: Entry) {
        items.append(entry)
        var child = items.count - 1
        while child > 0 {
            let parent = (child - 1) / 2
            guard items[child].cost < items[parent].cost else { break }
            items.swapAt(child, parent)
            child = parent
        }
    }

    mutating func pop() -> Entry? {
        guard let last = items.popLast() else { return nil }
        guard !items.isEmpty else { return last }
        let top = items[0]
        items[0] = last
        var parent = 0
        while true {
            let (left, right) = (2 * parent + 1, 2 * parent + 2)
            var smallest = parent
            if left < items.count, items[left].cost < items[smallest].cost { smallest = left }
            if right < items.count, items[right].cost < items[smallest].cost { smallest = right }
            if smallest == parent { break }
            items.swapAt(parent, smallest)
            parent = smallest
        }
        return top
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Decimation/MeshDecimationState.swift`:

```swift
/// The working state of one decimation (`MeshDecimator`): positions in `Double`, a quadric per vertex, the triangles around each
/// vertex, and the heap of candidate collapses. Vertices on an open edge are pinned: they never move and never go.
struct MeshDecimationState {
    var position: [SIMD3<Double>]
    var quadric: [Quadric]
    var isAlive: [Bool]
    var isPinned: [Bool]
    /// Counts the changes to each vertex, so a heap entry made before a change is known to be stale.
    var stamp: [UInt32]
    /// The live triangles that use each vertex.
    var incident: [[Int32]]
    var corners: [SIMD3<Int32>]
    var isTriangleAlive: [Bool]
    var liveTriangles: Int
    var heap = EdgeHeap()
    var collapses = 0

    /// - Throws: `MeshDecimator.Failure.nonManifold` when an edge has more than two faces.
    init(positions: [SIMD3<Float>], triangles: [UInt32]) throws {
        position = positions.map { SIMD3<Double>($0) }
        let count = positions.count
        corners = []
        corners.reserveCapacity(triangles.count / 3)
        for base in stride(from: 0, to: triangles.count - 2, by: 3) {
            let (a, b, c) = (triangles[base], triangles[base + 1], triangles[base + 2])
            // A triangle with a repeated or missing vertex is no face.
            guard a != b, b != c, a != c, Int(max(a, b, c)) < count else { continue }
            corners.append(SIMD3(Int32(a), Int32(b), Int32(c)))
        }
        quadric = [Quadric](repeating: Quadric(), count: count)
        isAlive = [Bool](repeating: true, count: count)
        isPinned = [Bool](repeating: false, count: count)
        stamp = [UInt32](repeating: 0, count: count)
        incident = [[Int32]](repeating: [], count: count)
        isTriangleAlive = [Bool](repeating: true, count: corners.count)
        liveTriangles = corners.count
        for (index, triangle) in corners.enumerated() {
            let (a, b, c) = (Int(triangle.x), Int(triangle.y), Int(triangle.z))
            let plane = Quadric(triangle: position[a], position[b], position[c])
            quadric[a] += plane
            quadric[b] += plane
            quadric[c] += plane
            incident[a].append(Int32(index))
            incident[b].append(Int32(index))
            incident[c].append(Int32(index))
        }
        let edges = try pinOpenEdges()
        heap.reserve(edges.count)
        for (u, v) in edges { if let entry = candidate(u, v, limit: .infinity) { heap.push(entry) } }
    }

    /// Pins the ends of every edge with a single face and returns every distinct edge as (low, high).
    private mutating func pinOpenEdges() throws -> [(Int, Int)] {
        var keys: [UInt64] = []
        keys.reserveCapacity(corners.count * 3)
        for triangle in corners {
            for side in 0..<3 {
                let (a, b) = (UInt64(triangle[side]), UInt64(triangle[(side + 1) % 3]))
                keys.append(min(a, b) << 32 | max(a, b))
            }
        }
        keys.sort()
        var edges: [(Int, Int)] = []
        var overfull = 0
        var start = 0
        while start < keys.count {
            var end = start + 1
            while end < keys.count, keys[end] == keys[start] { end += 1 }
            let (low, high) = (Int(keys[start] >> 32), Int(keys[start] & 0xFFFF_FFFF))
            edges.append((low, high))
            if end - start == 1 {
                isPinned[low] = true
                isPinned[high] = true
            } else if end - start > 2 {
                overfull += 1
            }
            start = end
        }
        if overfull > 0 { throw MeshDecimator.Failure.nonManifold(edges: overfull) }
        return edges
    }

    /// Where collapsing the edge (u, v) puts the new vertex, and what that costs (the quadric error there), or `nil` when both ends
    /// are pinned. A pinned end stays where it is; otherwise the best of both ends, the middle and the quadric's own optimum when
    /// that is near the edge.
    func target(_ u: Int, _ v: Int) -> (point: SIMD3<Double>, cost: Double)? {
        if isPinned[u] && isPinned[v] { return nil }
        let sum = quadric[u] + quadric[v]
        let (pu, pv) = (position[u], position[v])
        if isPinned[u] { return (pu, sum.error(at: pu)) }
        if isPinned[v] { return (pv, sum.error(at: pv)) }
        var best = (point: pu, cost: sum.error(at: pu))
        func consider(_ point: SIMD3<Double>) {
            let cost = sum.error(at: point)
            if cost < best.cost { best = (point, cost) }
        }
        let middle = (pu + pv) / 2
        consider(pv)
        consider(middle)
        if let optimum = sum.optimum(), (optimum - middle).length <= 2 * (pu - pv).length { consider(optimum) }
        return best
    }

    /// A heap entry for collapsing (u, v), or `nil` when it can't be done within `limit` (the squared tolerance).
    func candidate(_ u: Int, _ v: Int, limit: Double) -> EdgeHeap.Entry? {
        guard let chosen = target(u, v), chosen.cost <= limit else { return nil }
        return EdgeHeap.Entry(cost: chosen.cost, u: Int32(u), v: Int32(v), stampU: stamp[u], stampV: stamp[v])
    }
}

extension Quadric {
    /// The quadric of the plane of a triangle; zero for a triangle with no area.
    init(triangle a: SIMD3<Double>, _ b: SIMD3<Double>, _ c: SIMD3<Double>) {
        let normal = (b - a).cross(c - a)
        let length = normal.length
        guard length > 1e-300 else {
            self.init()
            return
        }
        self.init(normal: normal / length, through: a)
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Decimation/MeshDecimationState+Collapse.swift`:

```swift
extension MeshDecimationState {
    private func uses(_ triangle: SIMD3<Int32>, _ vertex: Int32) -> Bool {
        triangle.x == vertex || triangle.y == vertex || triangle.z == vertex
    }

    /// The vertices that share a live triangle with `vertex`, other than `vertex` and `excluding`.
    private func neighbours(of vertex: Int, excluding: Int) -> [Int32] {
        var found: [Int32] = []
        for index in incident[vertex] where isTriangleAlive[Int(index)] {
            let triangle = corners[Int(index)]
            for corner in [triangle.x, triangle.y, triangle.z] where Int(corner) != vertex && Int(corner) != excluding {
                if !found.contains(corner) { found.append(corner) }
            }
        }
        return found
    }

    /// The link condition: the two ends of the edge share as many neighbours as the edge has faces. Otherwise collapsing it would pinch
    /// the surface into a non-manifold vertex or edge.
    func keepsTheSurfaceManifold(_ u: Int, _ v: Int) -> Bool {
        let faces = incident[u].filter { isTriangleAlive[Int($0)] && uses(corners[Int($0)], Int32(v)) }.count
        let uSide = neighbours(of: u, excluding: v)
        let shared = neighbours(of: v, excluding: u).filter { uSide.contains($0) }.count
        return shared == faces && faces > 0
    }

    /// Whether moving `moving` to `point` leaves every triangle around it, except those on the edge to `other`, facing roughly the way it
    /// did and with some area.
    func keepsFacing(moving: Int, other: Int, to point: SIMD3<Double>) -> Bool {
        let tag = Int32(moving)
        for index in incident[moving] where isTriangleAlive[Int(index)] {
            let triangle = corners[Int(index)]
            if uses(triangle, Int32(other)) { continue }
            let (a, b, c) = (position[Int(triangle.x)], position[Int(triangle.y)], position[Int(triangle.z)])
            let (na, nb, nc) = (triangle.x == tag ? point : a, triangle.y == tag ? point : b, triangle.z == tag ? point : c)
            let (before, after) = ((b - a).cross(c - a), (nb - na).cross(nc - na))
            let (lengthBefore, lengthAfter) = (before.length, after.length)
            if lengthBefore == 0 { continue }
            if lengthAfter <= 1e-9 * lengthBefore || before.dot(after) < 0.1 * lengthBefore * lengthAfter { return false }
        }
        return true
    }

    /// Collapses the edge: `drop` joins `keep`, which moves to `point`. The triangles on the edge go; the others that used `drop` use
    /// `keep`.
    mutating func collapse(keep: Int, drop: Int, to point: SIMD3<Double>) {
        let (kept, dropped) = (Int32(keep), Int32(drop))
        var around = incident[keep].filter { isTriangleAlive[Int($0)] && !uses(corners[Int($0)], dropped) }
        for index in incident[keep] where isTriangleAlive[Int(index)] && uses(corners[Int(index)], dropped) {
            isTriangleAlive[Int(index)] = false
            liveTriangles -= 1
            let triangle = corners[Int(index)]
            for corner in [triangle.x, triangle.y, triangle.z] where corner != kept && corner != dropped {
                incident[Int(corner)].removeAll { $0 == index }
            }
        }
        for index in incident[drop] where isTriangleAlive[Int(index)] {
            var triangle = corners[Int(index)]
            if triangle.x == dropped { triangle.x = kept } else if triangle.y == dropped { triangle.y = kept } else { triangle.z = kept }
            corners[Int(index)] = triangle
            around.append(index)
        }
        incident[keep] = around
        incident[drop] = []
        position[keep] = point
        quadric[keep] += quadric[drop]
        isPinned[keep] = isPinned[keep] || isPinned[drop]
        isAlive[drop] = false
        stamp[keep] &+= 1
        collapses += 1
    }

    /// Pushes a fresh candidate for every edge at `vertex`, after it changed.
    mutating func requeue(around vertex: Int, limit: Double) {
        for other in neighbours(of: vertex, excluding: vertex) {
            if let entry = candidate(vertex, Int(other), limit: limit) { heap.push(entry) }
        }
    }

    /// Collapses the cheapest legal edges until none is left within `tolerance`, or the mesh is down to a tetrahedron.
    mutating func run(tolerance: Double) throws {
        let limit = tolerance * tolerance
        var steps = 0
        while liveTriangles > 4, let entry = heap.pop() {
            steps += 1
            if steps & 0xFFF == 0 { try Task.checkCancellation() }
            let (u, v) = (Int(entry.u), Int(entry.v))
            guard isAlive[u], isAlive[v], stamp[u] == entry.stampU, stamp[v] == entry.stampV,
                  let chosen = target(u, v), chosen.cost <= limit else { continue }
            guard keepsTheSurfaceManifold(u, v), keepsFacing(moving: u, other: v, to: chosen.point),
                  keepsFacing(moving: v, other: u, to: chosen.point) else { continue }
            let (keep, drop) = isPinned[v] ? (v, u) : (u, v)
            collapse(keep: keep, drop: drop, to: chosen.point)
            requeue(around: keep, limit: limit)
        }
    }

    /// The surviving vertices and triangles, renumbered in order of first use.
    func output() -> MeshDecimator.Output {
        var remap = [Int32](repeating: -1, count: position.count)
        var positions: [SIMD3<Float>] = []
        var origin: [Int32] = []
        var triangles: [UInt32] = []
        triangles.reserveCapacity(liveTriangles * 3)
        for (index, triangle) in corners.enumerated() where isTriangleAlive[index] {
            for vertex in [Int(triangle.x), Int(triangle.y), Int(triangle.z)] {
                if remap[vertex] < 0 {
                    remap[vertex] = Int32(positions.count)
                    positions.append(SIMD3<Float>(position[vertex]))
                    origin.append(Int32(vertex))
                }
                triangles.append(UInt32(remap[vertex]))
            }
        }
        return MeshDecimator.Output(positions: positions, triangles: triangles, origin: origin, collapses: collapses)
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Decimation/MeshDecimator.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation

/// Quadric error decimation (Garland and Heckbert; implicit spec §4.5): edge collapses in order of cost, from a priority queue. A
/// collapse is refused when it would make a vertex or an edge non-manifold (the link condition), flip a face, or cost more than the
/// tolerance. The cost is the quadric error at the new vertex, a sum of squared distances to the planes of every triangle the vertex
/// has absorbed, so its square root bounds the distance from the new vertex to each of those planes and the surface never moves
/// further than `tolerance` from where it was (a flat patch of k triangles costs √k times its noise, so the bound is a little
/// conservative on noisy flat areas). Vertices on an open edge are pinned: a mesh that is only a piece of a surface (one window of a
/// streamed export) keeps its edge exactly, and the piece beside it fits. A closed mesh stops at a tetrahedron.
public enum MeshDecimator {
    public struct Output: Sendable {
        public var positions: [SIMD3<Float>]
        public var triangles: [UInt32]
        /// For each output vertex, the input vertex it stands for. A vertex that never moved keeps its exact position.
        public var origin: [Int32]
        public var collapses: Int
    }

    public enum Failure: Error, Equatable, Sendable {
        /// An edge has more than two faces.
        case nonManifold(edges: Int)

        public var userMessage: String {
            switch self {
            case .nonManifold(let edges):
                "The mesh has \(edges.formatted(.number.locale(.messages))) edges with more than two faces, so it can't be simplified."
            }
        }
    }

    /// - Parameters:
    ///   - tolerance: the most a collapse may move the surface, in the mesh's units; 0 collapses only what is exactly flat.
    /// - Throws: `Failure.nonManifold` and `CancellationError`.
    public static func decimate(positions: [SIMD3<Float>], triangles: [UInt32], tolerance: Double) throws -> Output {
        var state = try MeshDecimationState(positions: positions, triangles: triangles)
        try state.run(tolerance: max(tolerance, 0))
        return state.output()
    }
}
```

### Task 2: The Decimate node and what every mesh tool shares

Decimate (spec §3.6): `mesh`, `tolerance` (mm, default 0.01) → `mesh`. What the five mesh tools share is built here once:

- `TriangleMesh.extraFacts` (inspector lines a tool adds: spec §5.2 "Decimate: triangles before → after", "Checks: the thinnest wall or the void
  count"), `derived(...)` (the geometry a tool made: a **new ID**, the resolution and quality of its input, **no `source`**) and
  `checked(...)` (the same geometry, flagged, a new ID). A new ID matters twice: `MeshSolids` caches the display solid by mesh ID, and
  the progressive preview refines by `source`; a tool's output that kept the input's ID or `source` would show the unchanged mesh or be
  replaced by a refinement of the *unprocessed* implicit. So **a tool's preview is the Mesh node's first pass** (or its fixed preview
  resolution), never refined (see User decisions); the badge still says "Preview …".
- `MeshInput.mesh(...)`: the one mesh a scalar is (an implicit wired in straight is meshed at the Mesh node's defaults, spec §2.3). Task 9
  replaces it with a stream-aware version.
- The library: `NodeCategory.mesh` gains Decimate. `OutputNodeTests` pins the totals (78, mesh 2) and `ImplicitLibraryTests` the names.

**Files:**
- Create: `Sources/CreatorNodes/Implicit/MeshTools/DecimateNode.swift`, `MeshInput+Single.swift`;

- Modify: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/TriangleMesh.swift`, `Sources/CreatorNodes/BuiltInNodes.swift`,

```swift
import CreatorGeometry

/// Decimate (implicit spec §3.6, §4.5).
struct DecimateNodeTests {
    /// A box with rounded edges, meshed by the Mesh node at 0.5 mm: lots of flat triangles on its faces.
    func decimated(_ h: inout Harness, tolerance: Double = 0.05) -> (mesh: Node, decimate: Node) {
        let box = h.add(ImplicitBoxNode.self, ["size": .vector(Vector3(12, 8, 6))])
        let mesh = h.add(MeshNode.self, ["preview resolution": .number(0.5)])
        let decimate = h.add(DecimateNode.self, ["tolerance": .number(tolerance)])
        h.wire(box, "implicit", to: mesh, "implicit")
        h.wire(mesh, "mesh", to: decimate, "mesh")
        return (mesh, decimate)
    }

    @Test func aBoxLosesItsFlatTrianglesAndStaysAClosedManifold() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let (mesh, decimate) = decimated(&h)
        let report = try await h.run([decimate])
        let before = try #require(report.triangleMesh(mesh)), after = try #require(report.triangleMesh(decimate))
        #expect(after.triangleCount < before.triangleCount / 4, "\(before.triangleCount) → \(after.triangleCount)")
        let topology = MeshTopology(after)
        #expect(topology.isClosedManifold && topology.eulerCharacteristic == 2)
        #expect(abs(after.signedVolume - before.signedVolume) / before.signedVolume < 0.01)
        #expect(after.id != before.id && after.source == nil && after.resolution == before.resolution && after.quality == before.quality)
        #expect(report.isOK(decimate))
    }

    @Test func theInspectorShowsTrianglesBeforeAndAfter() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let (mesh, decimate) = decimated(&h)
        let report = try await h.run([decimate])
        let before = try #require(report.triangleMesh(mesh)), after = try #require(report.triangleMesh(decimate))
        let fact = try #require(after.inspectorFacts.first { $0.label == "Decimated" })
        let was = before.triangleCount.formatted(.number.locale(.messages)), now = after.triangleCount.formatted(.number.locale(.messages))
        #expect(fact.value == "\(was) → \(now) triangles")
        #expect(after.inspectorFacts.last?.label == "Memory")
    }

    @Test func anImplicitWiredStraightInIsMeshedAtTheDefaultsFirst() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let sphere = h.add(ImplicitSphereNode.self, ["radius": .number(6)])
        let decimate = h.add(DecimateNode.self)
        h.wire(sphere, "implicit", to: decimate, "mesh")
        let report = try await h.run([decimate])
        let made = try #require(report.triangleMesh(decimate))
        #expect(MeshTopology(made).isClosedManifold && made.triangleCount > 100)
    }

    @Test func aToleranceThatIsTooSmallOrNotANumberIsRefused() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let (_, small) = decimated(&h, tolerance: 0.00001)
        let (_, nan) = decimated(&h, tolerance: .nan)
        let report = try await h.run([small, nan])
        #expect(report.error(small) == "“tolerance” must be at least 0.0001 mm.")
        #expect(report.error(nan) != nil)
    }

    @Test func aBiggerToleranceKeepsFewerTriangles() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let sphere = h.add(ImplicitSphereNode.self, ["radius": .number(6)])
        let mesh = h.add(MeshNode.self, ["preview resolution": .number(0.4)])
        let tight = h.add(DecimateNode.self, ["tolerance": .number(0.002)])
        let loose = h.add(DecimateNode.self, ["tolerance": .number(0.05)])
        h.wire(sphere, "implicit", to: mesh, "implicit")
        for node in [tight, loose] { h.wire(mesh, "mesh", to: node, "mesh") }
        let report = try await h.run([tight, loose])
        let (a, b) = (try #require(report.triangleMesh(tight)), try #require(report.triangleMesh(loose)))
        #expect(b.triangleCount < a.triangleCount && MeshTopology(b).isClosedManifold)
    }
}
```

```swift
    /// This mesh with other highlights (checks return their input flagged).
    public func highlighting(_ highlights: [MeshHighlight]) -> TriangleMesh {
        var copy = self
        copy.highlights = highlights
        return copy
    }

    /// A mesh a tool made from this one (Decimate): the new geometry, a new ID, the resolution and quality of this mesh, and no
    /// `source` (the progressive preview refines what a Mesh node made, never what a tool changed). The tool's own lines for the
    /// inspector replace this mesh's.
    public func derived(positions: [SIMD3<Float>], normals: [SIMD3<Float>] = [], triangles: [UInt32],
                        facts: [PayloadFact] = []) -> TriangleMesh {
        TriangleMesh(positions: positions, normals: normals, triangles: triangles, resolution: resolution, quality: quality,
                     highlights: [], source: nil, meshingSeconds: meshingSeconds, extraFacts: facts)
    }

    /// The same geometry after a check (Check Thin Walls, Check Trapped Volumes): a new ID, so the viewport shows the flagged mesh
    /// and not the one it came from, with the flagged triangles, and no `source`.
    public func checked(highlights: [MeshHighlight], facts: [PayloadFact]) -> TriangleMesh {
        TriangleMesh(positions: positions, normals: normals, triangles: triangles, resolution: resolution, quality: quality,
                     highlights: highlights, source: nil, meshingSeconds: meshingSeconds, extraFacts: extraFacts + facts)
    }
}
```

```swift
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorImplicit

extension MeshInput {
    /// The one mesh a value is, and the warnings of making it: a mesh as it is, an implicit solid meshed at the Mesh node's defaults.
    /// The mesh tools (Decimate, the checks, Mesh to Solid) take their input this way.
    static func mesh(_ scalar: Scalar, socket: SocketName, context: EvalContext) async throws -> (mesh: TriangleMesh, warnings: [String]) {
        let made = try await meshes([scalar], socket: socket, context: context)
        guard let mesh = made.meshes.first else { throw NodeError.typeMismatch(socket, expected: .mesh) }
        return (mesh, made.warnings)
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorNodes/Implicit/MeshTools/DecimateNode.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation

/// Fewer triangles for the same shape (implicit spec §3.6, §4.5): flat areas collapse into big triangles by quadric error edge
/// collapse, and the surface moves by no more than `tolerance`. The result is still a closed manifold. Its triangles are shown as
/// "before → after" in the inspector.
public enum DecimateNode: NodeDefinition {
    public static let typeID = "creator.decimate"
    public static let displayName = "Decimate"
    public static let category = NodeCategory.mesh
    public static let defaultTolerance = 0.01
    public static let minimumTolerance = 0.0001
    public static let inputs = [
        SocketSpec("mesh", .mesh),
        SocketSpec("tolerance", .number, defaultValue: .number(defaultTolerance), unit: .millimetres, range: minimumTolerance...1),
    ]
    public static let outputs = [SocketSpec("mesh", .mesh)]
    public static let inspector = [
        InspectorSection(title: "Decimate", controls: [
            .number("tolerance"),
            .note("Flat areas become big triangles. The surface moves by no more than the tolerance."),
        ]),
    ]

    public static func evaluate(_ inputs: NodeInputs, kernel: any Kernel, context: EvalContext) async throws -> NodeOutputs {
        let tolerance = try inputs.number("tolerance")
        guard tolerance.isFinite, tolerance >= minimumTolerance else {
            let smallest = minimumTolerance.formatted(.number.precision(.fractionLength(0...4)).locale(.messages))
            throw NodeError.invalidValue("“tolerance” must be at least \(smallest) mm.")
        }
        let (input, warnings) = try await MeshInput.mesh(try inputs.scalar("mesh"), socket: "mesh", context: context)
        let output: MeshDecimator.Output
        do {
            output = try MeshDecimator.decimate(positions: input.positions, triangles: input.triangles, tolerance: tolerance)
        } catch let failure as MeshDecimator.Failure {
            throw NodeError.invalidValue(failure.userMessage)
        }
        let (before, after) = (input.triangleCount, output.triangles.count / 3)
        let note = "\(before.formatted(.number.locale(.messages))) → \(after.formatted(.number.locale(.messages))) triangles"
        let made = input.derived(positions: output.positions, triangles: output.triangles, facts: [PayloadFact("Decimated", note)])
        return NodeOutputs(["mesh": .mesh(made)], warnings: warnings)
    }
}
```

### Task 3: Check Thin Walls

Spec §4.6: *per vertex, a ray inward along −normal against the mesh's BVH; thickness is the first hit distance. Below `minimum`, the vertex's
faces are highlighted; nearby flagged faces are grouped into "places".* Warning, exactly: "Walls thinner than 0.4 mm in N places (thinnest
0.21 mm)." and, on a preview-quality mesh, "(approximate: preview resolution)" in it.

Four decisions the spec leaves open, all pinned by tests:

1. **The far side must face back.** A hit counts only on a triangle whose normal opposes the vertex's (dot < −0.5, within 60 degrees).
   Without it, a vertex near a sharp convex edge of a plain box would "see" the adjacent face a hair away and flag every edge of every part
   (`aMeshOfABoxFromTheMesherHasNoFalsePlacesAtItsEdges`). The triangles the vertex belongs to are skipped.
2. **Places** are the connected groups of flagged triangles (sharing a vertex), *and* the two sides of one wall: a flagged vertex is joined
   to the vertex of the triangle its ray hit. A hollow cube's thin wall is one place, not two shells' worth; two separate plates are two.
3. **The ray is only as long as `minimum`.** Nothing farther is a thin wall, so the BVH walk ends early and a part with no thin wall costs
   little. The price is that "thinnest" is the thinnest *below the minimum*; a clear mesh's inspector line says "Thin walls: none under 0.4 mm".
4. **Wording.** One place is "1 place" (not "1 places"); the label sits before the full stop: "… (thinnest 0.21 mm) (approximate: preview
   resolution)." (see User decisions).

The node outputs the same mesh (a new ID, no `source`) with `highlights` (the flagged triangles, label "Walls thinner than 0.4 mm") and the
inspector line "Thinnest wall: 0.21 mm". The viewport tint comes in Task 10.

Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/Support/MeshFixtures+Hollow.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorImplicit

extension MeshFixtures {
    /// A cube of `size` with cube-shaped voids inside it: closed shells, the voids wound the other way (their faces look into
    /// them). Each void is (x, y, z, side) from the cube's corner. Each face has `divisions` squares along an edge, so a vertex has
    /// whatever lies straight behind it.
    static func cubeWithVoids(size: Float, divisions: Int, voids: [SIMD4<Float>]) -> TriangleMesh {
        let unit = subdividedCube(divisions: divisions)
        var positions = unit.positions.map { $0 * size }
        var triangles = unit.triangles
        for void in voids {
            let offset = UInt32(positions.count)
            positions += unit.positions.map { $0 * void.w + SIMD3(void.x, void.y, void.z) }
            for base in stride(from: 0, to: unit.triangles.count, by: 3) {
                // Reversed, so the void's faces look into it.
                triangles += [unit.triangles[base] + offset, unit.triangles[base + 2] + offset, unit.triangles[base + 1] + offset]
            }
        }
        return TriangleMesh(positions: positions, triangles: triangles)
    }

    /// A cube of `size` with one cube-shaped void in the middle, a wall of `wall` all round.
    static func hollowCube(size: Float, wall: Float, divisions: Int) -> TriangleMesh {
        cubeWithVoids(size: size, divisions: divisions, voids: [SIMD4(wall, wall, wall, size - 2 * wall)])
    }
}
```

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

/// Check Thin Walls (implicit spec §4.6, §7): a 0.2 mm wall is found, a sound part is not.
struct ThinWallCheckTests {
    @Test func aWallOfAFifthOfAMillimetreIsFoundWhereverTheCubeHasOne() throws {
        let hollow = MeshFixtures.hollowCube(size: 10, wall: 0.2, divisions: 10)
        let result = try ThinWallCheck.run(hollow, minimum: 0.4)
        #expect(abs((result.thinnest ?? 0) - 0.2) < 1e-6, "thinnest \(String(describing: result.thinnest))")
        #expect(result.places == 1, "the two sides of one wall are one place; got \(result.places)")
        #expect(result.triangles.count == hollow.triangleCount, "every face of both shells has a thin wall behind it")
    }

    @Test func aWallThickerThanTheMinimumIsLeftAlone() throws {
        let hollow = MeshFixtures.hollowCube(size: 10, wall: 0.5, divisions: 10)
        let result = try ThinWallCheck.run(hollow, minimum: 0.4)
        #expect(result.triangles.isEmpty && result.places == 0 && result.thinnest == nil)
    }

    @Test func aSolidCubeAndASphereHaveNoThinWalls() throws {
        let cube = MeshFixtures.subdividedCube(divisions: 8)
        #expect(try ThinWallCheck.run(cube, minimum: 0.4).places == 0)
        #expect(try ThinWallCheck.run(MeshFixtures.sphereMesh(radius: 5, subdivisions: 3), minimum: 0.4).places == 0)
    }

    @Test func aSharpCornerIsNotAWall() throws {
        // The faces of a cube meet at 90 degrees; the face beside a vertex near an edge is not the far side of a wall.
        let small = MeshFixtures.subdividedCube(divisions: 20)
        let scaled = TriangleMesh(positions: small.positions.map { $0 * 2 }, triangles: small.triangles)
        #expect(try ThinWallCheck.run(scaled, minimum: 0.4).places == 0)
    }

    @Test func twoThinPlatesApartAreTwoPlaces() throws {
        let one = MeshFixtures.hollowCube(size: 10, wall: 0.2, divisions: 6)
        let shift = SIMD3<Float>(30, 0, 0)
        let both = TriangleMesh(positions: one.positions + one.positions.map { $0 + shift },
                                triangles: one.triangles + one.triangles.map { $0 + UInt32(one.positions.count) })
        let result = try ThinWallCheck.run(both, minimum: 0.4)
        #expect(result.places == 2)
    }

    @Test func aMeshFromTheMesherHasTheWallItWasGiven() async throws {
        // A ball with a hollow centre: a shell 0.3 mm thick, meshed at 0.1 mm.
        let shell = ImplicitSolid.shell(.sphere(radius: 2), thickness: .constant(0.3), side: .inward)
        let mesh = try await ImplicitMesher(evaluator: CPUBatchEvaluator(shell)).mesh(shell, resolution: 0.1, quality: .export)
        let result = try ThinWallCheck.run(mesh, minimum: 0.45)
        #expect(result.places == 1 && result.triangles.count > mesh.triangleCount / 2)
        let thinnest = try #require(result.thinnest)
        #expect(abs(thinnest - 0.3) < 0.05, "thinnest \(thinnest)")
        #expect(try ThinWallCheck.run(mesh, minimum: 0.2).places == 0)
    }

    @Test func aMeshOfABoxFromTheMesherHasNoFalsePlacesAtItsEdges() async throws {
        let box = ImplicitSolid.box(halfSize: Vector3(3, 2, 2), cornerRadius: 0)
        let mesh = try await ImplicitMesher(evaluator: CPUBatchEvaluator(box)).mesh(box, resolution: 0.15, quality: .export)
        #expect(try ThinWallCheck.run(mesh, minimum: 0.4).places == 0)
    }

    @Test func anEmptyMeshOrABadMinimumFindsNothing() throws {
        let empty = TriangleMesh(positions: [], triangles: [])
        #expect(try ThinWallCheck.run(empty, minimum: 0.4).places == 0)
        #expect(try ThinWallCheck.run(MeshFixtures.cube, minimum: .nan).places == 0)
        #expect(try ThinWallCheck.run(MeshFixtures.cube, minimum: 0).places == 0)
    }
}
```

```swift
import CreatorGeometry

/// Check Thin Walls (implicit spec §3.6): the highlight, the exact warning, and the label on a preview.
struct CheckThinWallsNodeTests {
    /// A hollow ball with a 0.3 mm wall as an implicit solid, meshed at the given quality by the Mesh node, then checked.
    func checked(_ h: inout Harness, minimum: Double = 0.4, exportResolution: Double = 0.1) -> (mesh: Node, check: Node) {
        let sphere = h.add(ImplicitSphereNode.self, ["radius": .number(2)])
        let shell = h.add(ImplicitShellNode.self, ["thickness": .number(0.3)])
        let mesh = h.add(MeshNode.self, ["export resolution": .number(exportResolution)])
        let check = h.add(CheckThinWallsNode.self, ["minimum": .number(minimum)])
        h.wire(sphere, "implicit", to: shell, "implicit")
        h.wire(shell, "implicit", to: mesh, "implicit")
        h.wire(mesh, "mesh", to: check, "mesh")
        return (mesh, check)
    }

    @Test func aThinWallIsHighlightedAndWarnedAboutInTheSpecsWords() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let (mesh, check) = checked(&h)
        let report = try await h.runForExport([check])
        let made = try #require(report.triangleMesh(check)), source = try #require(report.triangleMesh(mesh))
        let highlight = try #require(made.highlights.first)
        #expect(made.highlights.count == 1 && !highlight.triangles.isEmpty && highlight.label == "Walls thinner than 0.4 mm")
        #expect(made.positions == source.positions && made.triangles == source.triangles && made.id != source.id)
        let message = try #require(report.warning(check))
        #expect(message.hasPrefix("Walls thinner than 0.4 mm in 1 place (thinnest 0.3"), "\(message)")
        #expect(message.hasSuffix(" mm).") && !message.contains("approximate"))
        #expect(made.inspectorFacts.contains { $0.label == "Thinnest wall" })
    }

    @Test func aPreviewSaysItIsApproximate() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let (_, check) = checked(&h)
        let report = try await h.run([check])
        let message = try #require(report.warning(check))
        #expect(message.hasSuffix(" mm) (approximate: preview resolution)."), "\(message)")
    }

    @Test func aWallAboveTheMinimumIsClear() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let (_, check) = checked(&h, minimum: 0.2)
        let report = try await h.runForExport([check])
        let made = try #require(report.triangleMesh(check))
        #expect(made.highlights.isEmpty && report.isOK(check))
        #expect(made.inspectorFacts.contains(PayloadFact("Thin walls", "none under 0.2 mm")))
    }

    @Test func theMinimumIsChecked() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let (_, small) = checked(&h, minimum: 0.001)
        let (_, nan) = checked(&h, minimum: .nan)
        let report = try await h.run([small, nan])
        #expect(report.error(small) == "“minimum” must be at least 0.01 mm.")
        #expect(report.error(nan) != nil)
    }

    @Test func thePluralsAndTheLabelAreTheSpecsWords() {
        #expect(CheckWording.thinWalls(minimum: 0.4, places: 3, thinnest: 0.21, label: nil)
            == "Walls thinner than 0.4 mm in 3 places (thinnest 0.21 mm).")
        #expect(CheckWording.thinWalls(minimum: 0.4, places: 1, thinnest: 0.21, label: nil)
            == "Walls thinner than 0.4 mm in 1 place (thinnest 0.21 mm).")
        #expect(CheckWording.thinWalls(minimum: 0.4, places: 1_200, thinnest: 0.21, label: "approximate: preview resolution")
            == "Walls thinner than 0.4 mm in 1,200 places (thinnest 0.21 mm) (approximate: preview resolution).")
    }
}
```
