Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Bake/TriangleBVH+Ray.swift`:

```swift
extension TriangleBVH {
    /// The nearest hit of a ray, or `nil` when none is within `limit`. `test(triangle)` returns the distance along the ray at which it
    /// meets that triangle, or `nil`; the walk skips boxes the ray does not reach within the best hit found so far.
    func nearestHit(origin: SIMD3<Double>, direction: SIMD3<Double>, limit: Double, test: (Int) -> Double?) -> Double? {
        guard !nodes.isEmpty else { return nil }
        // A zero component would make 0 × ∞ = NaN in the slab test, so it is a tiny one.
        let safe = SIMD3(direction.x == 0 ? 1e-300 : direction.x, direction.y == 0 ? 1e-300 : direction.y,
                         direction.z == 0 ? 1e-300 : direction.z)
        let inverse = SIMD3<Double>(1, 1, 1) / safe
        var best = limit
        var found = false
        var stack = [0]
        stack.reserveCapacity(64)
        while let index = stack.popLast() {
            let node = nodes[index]
            guard reaches(node, origin: origin, inverse: inverse, within: best) else { continue }
            if node.count > 0 {
                for slot in Int(node.first)..<Int(node.first + node.count) {
                    if let distance = test(Int(order[slot])), distance < best {
                        best = distance
                        found = true
                    }
                }
            } else {
                stack.append(Int(node.first))
                stack.append(Int(node.second))
            }
        }
        return found ? best : nil
    }

    /// The slab test: whether the ray enters the node's box at a distance of at most `limit`.
    private func reaches(_ node: Node, origin: SIMD3<Double>, inverse: SIMD3<Double>, within limit: Double) -> Bool {
        let (a, b) = ((node.low - origin) * inverse, (node.high - origin) * inverse)
        let (near, far) = (pointwiseMin(a, b), pointwiseMax(a, b))
        let entry = max(near.x, near.y, near.z, 0)
        let exit = min(far.x, far.y, far.z)
        return entry <= exit && entry <= limit
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Checks/MeshVertexNormals.swift`:

```swift
/// Unit normals at the vertices of a mesh for the checks: the mesher's own (from the gradient of the implicit) where it set one, else the
/// average of the faces around the vertex weighted by area.
enum MeshVertexNormals {
    static func of(_ mesh: TriangleMesh) -> [SIMD3<Double>] {
        var sums = [SIMD3<Double>](repeating: .zero, count: mesh.vertexCount)
        let points = mesh.positions.map { SIMD3<Double>($0) }
        for base in stride(from: 0, to: mesh.triangles.count - 2, by: 3) {
            let corners = (0..<3).map { Int(mesh.triangles[base + $0]) }
            let normal = (points[corners[1]] - points[corners[0]]).cross(points[corners[2]] - points[corners[0]])
            for corner in corners { sums[corner] += normal }
        }
        let own = mesh.normals.count == mesh.vertexCount
        return (0..<mesh.vertexCount).map { index in
            if own {
                let given = SIMD3<Double>(mesh.normals[index])
                let length = given.length
                if length > 0.5, length.isFinite { return given / length }
            }
            let length = sums[index].length
            return length > 0 ? sums[index] / length : .zero
        }
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Checks/VertexClusters.swift`:

```swift
/// Union-find over vertex numbers: which vertices belong to the same place.
struct VertexClusters {
    private var parent: [Int32]

    init(count: Int) {
        parent = (0..<count).map(Int32.init)
    }

    mutating func root(of vertex: Int) -> Int {
        var current = vertex
        while Int(parent[current]) != current {
            parent[current] = parent[Int(parent[current])]
            current = Int(parent[current])
        }
        return current
    }

    mutating func join(_ a: Int, _ b: Int) {
        let (rootA, rootB) = (root(of: a), root(of: b))
        if rootA != rootB { parent[max(rootA, rootB)] = Int32(min(rootA, rootB)) }
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Checks/ThinWallCheck.swift`:

```swift
/// Thin walls (implicit spec §4.6): from each vertex a ray goes inwards, against the normal, and the first surface it meets that faces
/// back at it is the other side of the wall. A vertex whose wall is thinner than `minimum` is flagged, with its faces. Flagged faces
/// that touch, and the two sides of one wall, make one place. A surface that meets the ray edge-on (the next face of a sharp corner) is
/// not the far side of a wall and is passed over, as is any triangle the vertex belongs to.
public struct ThinWallCheck: Sendable {
    public struct Result: Sendable {
        /// The triangles with a flagged vertex, in order.
        public var triangles: [Int]
        /// How many separate places they make.
        public var places: Int
        /// The thinnest wall found, or `nil` when none is thinner than `minimum`.
        public var thinnest: Double?
    }

    /// A far side must face back against the vertex normal within 60 degrees.
    static let facingLimit = -0.5

    private let points: [SIMD3<Double>]
    private let corners: [SIMD3<Int32>]
    private let normals: [SIMD3<Double>]
    private let faceNormals: [SIMD3<Double>]
    private let bvh: TriangleBVH

    private init(_ mesh: TriangleMesh) {
        points = mesh.positions.map { SIMD3<Double>($0) }
        corners = (0..<mesh.triangleCount).map {
            SIMD3(Int32(mesh.triangles[3 * $0]), Int32(mesh.triangles[3 * $0 + 1]), Int32(mesh.triangles[3 * $0 + 2]))
        }
        let points = points
        faceNormals = corners.map { triangle in
            let normal = (points[Int(triangle.y)] - points[Int(triangle.x)]).cross(points[Int(triangle.z)] - points[Int(triangle.x)])
            let length = normal.length
            return length > 0 ? normal / length : .zero
        }
        normals = MeshVertexNormals.of(mesh)
        bvh = TriangleBVH(vertices: points, triangles: corners)
    }

    /// How far the wall behind `vertex` is, and the triangle that is its far side, when it is thinner than `limit`.
    private func wall(behind vertex: Int, limit: Double) -> (distance: Double, triangle: Int)? {
        var hitTriangle = -1
        let direction = -normals[vertex]
        let distance = bvh.nearestHit(origin: points[vertex], direction: direction, limit: limit) { index in
            let triangle = corners[index]
            guard Int(triangle.x) != vertex, Int(triangle.y) != vertex, Int(triangle.z) != vertex,
                  faceNormals[index].dot(normals[vertex]) < Self.facingLimit,
                  let t = Self.hit(points[vertex], direction, points[Int(triangle.x)], points[Int(triangle.y)], points[Int(triangle.z)]),
                  t > 1e-9 else { return nil }
            hitTriangle = index
            return t
        }
        guard let distance, distance < limit, hitTriangle >= 0 else { return nil }
        return (distance, hitTriangle)
    }

    /// - Throws: `CancellationError`.
    public static func run(_ mesh: TriangleMesh, minimum: Double) throws -> Result {
        guard mesh.triangleCount > 0, minimum.isFinite, minimum > 0 else { return Result(triangles: [], places: 0, thinnest: nil) }
        let scan = ThinWallCheck(mesh)
        var clusters = VertexClusters(count: scan.points.count)
        var flagged = [Bool](repeating: false, count: scan.points.count)
        var thinnest = Double.infinity
        let used = Set(scan.corners.flatMap { [Int($0.x), Int($0.y), Int($0.z)] })
        for (count, vertex) in used.sorted().enumerated() where scan.normals[vertex] != .zero {
            if count & 0xFFF == 0 { try Task.checkCancellation() }
            guard let found = scan.wall(behind: vertex, limit: minimum) else { continue }
            flagged[vertex] = true
            thinnest = min(thinnest, found.distance)
            // The two sides of a wall are one place.
            clusters.join(vertex, Int(scan.corners[found.triangle].x))
        }
        var triangles: [Int] = []
        var anchors: [Int] = []
        for (index, triangle) in scan.corners.enumerated() {
            let marked = [Int(triangle.x), Int(triangle.y), Int(triangle.z)].filter { flagged[$0] }
            guard let first = marked.first else { continue }
            triangles.append(index)
            anchors.append(first)
            for other in marked.dropFirst() { clusters.join(first, other) }
        }
        let places = Set(anchors.map { clusters.root(of: $0) }).count
        return Result(triangles: triangles, places: places, thinnest: triangles.isEmpty ? nil : thinnest)
    }

    /// Möller–Trumbore: the distance along the ray at which it crosses the triangle, or `nil`.
    static func hit(_ origin: SIMD3<Double>, _ direction: SIMD3<Double>, _ a: SIMD3<Double>, _ b: SIMD3<Double>,
                    _ c: SIMD3<Double>) -> Double? {
        let (edge1, edge2) = (b - a, c - a)
        let pvec = direction.cross(edge2)
        let determinant = edge1.dot(pvec)
        guard abs(determinant) > 1e-14 else { return nil }
        let inverse = 1 / determinant
        let tvec = origin - a
        let u = tvec.dot(pvec) * inverse
        guard u >= 0, u <= 1 else { return nil }
        let qvec = tvec.cross(edge1)
        let v = direction.dot(qvec) * inverse
        guard v >= 0, u + v <= 1 else { return nil }
        return edge2.dot(qvec) * inverse
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorNodes/Implicit/MeshTools/CheckWording.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation

/// The words of the mesh checks' warnings (implicit spec §3.6), written in `Locale.messages`.
enum CheckWording {
    /// The label a check run on a coarse copy carries before the full stop (implicit spec §3.6, Errata D12).
    static let previewLabel = "approximate: preview resolution"

    /// "Walls thinner than 0.4 mm in 3 places (thinnest 0.21 mm)." (spec §3.6; one place is "1 place").
    static func thinWalls(minimum: Double, places: Int, thinnest: Double, label: String?) -> String {
        let count = places.formatted(.number.locale(.messages))
        let noun = places == 1 ? "place" : "places"
        return sentence("Walls thinner than \(minimum.millimetreText(locale: .messages)) mm in \(count) \(noun) "
            + "(thinnest \(thinnest.millimetreText(locale: .messages)) mm)", label: label)
    }

    /// "3 enclosed voids can't drain." (spec §3.6; one is "1 enclosed void can't drain.").
    static func voids(_ count: Int, label: String?) -> String {
        let number = count.formatted(.number.locale(.messages))
        return sentence(count == 1 ? "\(number) enclosed void can't drain" : "\(number) enclosed voids can't drain", label: label)
    }

    /// `text`, then the label in brackets when there is one, then the full stop.
    private static func sentence(_ text: String, label: String?) -> String {
        label.map { "\(text) (\($0))." } ?? "\(text)."
    }

    /// The label for a check of `mesh`: a preview mesh is coarse.
    static func label(for mesh: TriangleMesh) -> String? {
        mesh.quality == .preview ? previewLabel : nil
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorNodes/Implicit/MeshTools/CheckThinWallsNode.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation

/// Finds walls thinner than a minimum (implicit spec §3.6, §4.6): the mesh comes out as it went in, with the thin regions
/// highlighted in the viewport and a warning that counts the places and names the thinnest. On a preview mesh the warning says it is
/// approximate.
public enum CheckThinWallsNode: NodeDefinition {
    public static let typeID = "creator.checkThinWalls"
    public static let displayName = "Check Thin Walls"
    public static let category = NodeCategory.mesh
    public static let defaultMinimum = 0.4
    public static let minimumMinimum = 0.01
    public static let inputs = [
        SocketSpec("mesh", .mesh),
        SocketSpec("minimum", .number, defaultValue: .number(defaultMinimum), unit: .millimetres, range: minimumMinimum...5),
    ]
    public static let outputs = [SocketSpec("mesh", .mesh)]
    public static let inspector = [
        InspectorSection(title: "Check Thin Walls", controls: [
            .number("minimum"),
            .note("Regions thinner than the minimum are highlighted."),
        ]),
    ]

    public static func evaluate(_ inputs: NodeInputs, kernel: any Kernel, context: EvalContext) async throws -> NodeOutputs {
        let minimum = try inputs.number("minimum")
        guard minimum.isFinite, minimum >= minimumMinimum else {
            throw NodeError.invalidValue("“minimum” must be at least \(minimumMinimum.millimetreText(locale: .messages)) mm.")
        }
        let (mesh, warnings) = try await MeshInput.mesh(try inputs.scalar("mesh"), socket: "mesh", context: context)
        let result = try ThinWallCheck.run(mesh, minimum: minimum)
        let limit = minimum.millimetreText(locale: .messages)
        guard let thinnest = result.thinnest, !result.triangles.isEmpty else {
            let clear = mesh.checked(highlights: [], facts: [PayloadFact("Thin walls", "none under \(limit) mm")])
            return NodeOutputs(["mesh": .mesh(clear)], warnings: warnings)
        }
        let highlight = MeshHighlight(triangles: result.triangles, label: "Walls thinner than \(limit) mm")
        let flagged = mesh.checked(highlights: [highlight],
                                   facts: [PayloadFact("Thinnest wall", "\(thinnest.millimetreText(locale: .messages)) mm")])
        let text = CheckWording.thinWalls(minimum: minimum, places: result.places, thinnest: thinnest, label: CheckWording.label(for: mesh))
        return NodeOutputs(["mesh": .mesh(flagged)], warnings: warnings + [text])
    }
}
```

### Task 4: Check Trapped Volumes

Spec §4.6: *a coarse occupancy grid (2 mm or 32 cells across, whichever is finer) flood-filled from outside; unreached empty components
larger than one cell are voids.* Warning, exactly: "N enclosed voids can't drain." (one void: "1 enclosed void can't drain."; see User
decisions), with "(approximate: preview resolution)" before the full stop on a preview mesh.

- **The grid** (`OccupancyGrid`): cell = min(**0.5 mm**, longest side ÷ 32), made coarser only as far as keeps the grid within **32 million cells** (padding included; about ten
  bytes a cell in the flood fill, so some 300 MB). Two cells of padding all round, so the corner is certainly outside. **The spec says 2 mm, and a 2 mm cell cannot work with Drain Holes:**
  a hole of the default 2 mm is one cell across, and a cell beside its wall is "within half a cell of the surface" (below), so the grid sees the hole sealed and the Check Trapped Volumes
  that follows Drain Holes (Automatic) goes on reporting the void; the lightweight-bracket acceptance test (Task 12) ran into exactly this. A hole is seen when it is about four cells
  across, so the cell is at most 0.5 mm (`aDrainHoleOfTwoMillimetresIsSeenThroughAtTheDefaultCell`; see User decisions).
- **The large-part case, measured by `cellSize(forSize:)` and said out loud.** The budget holds 0.5 mm up to a 150 mm cube, and a plate a metre square and 10 mm thick gets about 0.75 mm
  (a per-axis cap of 256 cells, which this task had first, made every part over 128 mm coarser than 0.5 mm, a plate included). A 300 mm cube gets 1 mm cells and a metre cube 3 mm, where
  a 2 mm hole is again smaller than the grid can see through, so the check after Drain Holes can report a void that is drained. **That is a limit, not a fix**: the check adds a "Grid cell"
  fact to the mesh and, when the cell is above 0.5 mm, the warning "The part is large: the check's cells are 0.96 mm, so holes under 3.84 mm may not count as open." (a 300 mm cube) (`CheckWording.coarseGrid`);
  Drain Holes warns when its diameter is under four cells (Task 5). The check cannot size its grid from the Drain Holes in the document: a node sees its own inputs, and a mesh carries no
  record of how its implicit was made. Raising `largestCellCount` is the lever (memory grows with it). Recorded as an erratum to spec §4.6 (Task 12).
- **What is solid.** A cell is solid when its centre is inside the part *or within half a cell of the surface* (the signed distance ≤ cell/2). The second half
  is what keeps a wall **thinner than a cell** from being a gap: a 0.2 mm wall between two 0.31 mm cells still has a centre within 0.155 mm of it on some
  cell along every line crossing it (`aVoidInsideAThinWallIsStillSealed`). The price is that a void or a channel must be larger than about a cell to be
  seen, which is the spec's "larger than one cell".
- **Cost follows the surface, not the volume.** `fill` first classifies blocks of 8 × 8 × 8 cells by the distance at their middle (the signed distance is
  1-Lipschitz, a baked or composed implicit declares its own bound): a block whose middle is farther than its radius from the half-cell shell is all
  solid or all empty, and only the blocks the surface may cross are asked cell by cell (`fillingByBlocksGivesWhatFillingCellByCellGives` holds the result
  to the brute-force fill and counts the questions). A synchronous form takes a batch function (the mesh's `SignedDistanceMesh`); an `awaiting` form takes an
  `async` one (the GPU evaluator, Task 5).
- **`EnclosedVoids`**: flood fill (6-connected) from the corner; the empty cells left are grouped; pockets of one cell are dropped.
- **Highlight**: a triangle looks into a void when a void cell lies 0.75, 1.25 or 1.75 cells out along its normal.
- The same grid is built from an *implicit* by Task 5, so Drain Holes (Automatic) finds the voids the check would.
- A mesh that is not closed has no inside: the node says "This mesh isn't closed, so inside and outside are undefined."

**Files:**
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Checks/OccupancyGrid.swift`, `EnclosedVoids.swift`, `TrappedVolumeCheck.swift`,
  `Sources/CreatorNodes/Implicit/MeshTools/CheckTrappedVolumesNode.swift`
- Test: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/OccupancyGridTests.swift`, `TrappedVolumeCheckTests.swift`,
  `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/Implicit/CheckTrappedVolumesNodeTests.swift`
- Modify: `BuiltInNodes.swift`, `ImplicitLibraryTests.swift`, `OutputNodeTests.swift` (80; mesh 4)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `SignedDistanceMesh` (7c-core), `MeshFixtures.cubeWithVoids` (Task 3), `CheckWording.voids` (Task 3).
- Produces: `OccupancyGrid(bounds:)` (`cell`, `counts`, `isSolid`, `index(_:_:_:)`, `coordinates(of:)`, `center(of:)`, `index(containing:)`,
  `setSolid(_:at:)`, `fill(from:)`); `EnclosedVoids(_:) throws` (`label`, `voids`); `TrappedVolumeCheck.run(_:) throws -> Result(voidCount, triangles, cell)`;
  `OccupancyGrid.cellSize(forSize:)`, `smallestSeenHole(forSize:)`, `largestCell`, `largestCellCount`; `CheckWording.coarseGrid(cell:)`; `CheckTrappedVolumesNode` (`creator.checkTrappedVolumes`).

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

/// The coarse grid and the flood fill behind Check Trapped Volumes and Drain Holes (implicit spec §4.6).
struct OccupancyGridTests {
    @Test func theCellIsHalfAMillimetreOrAThirtySecondOfTheLongestSideWhicheverIsFiner() {
        #expect(OccupancyGrid.cellSize(forSize: Vector3(10, 10, 10)) == 10.0 / 32)
        #expect(OccupancyGrid.cellSize(forSize: Vector3(16, 3, 3)) == 0.5)
        #expect(OccupancyGrid.cellSize(forSize: Vector3(100, 100, 100)) == 0.5)
        #expect(OccupancyGrid.cellSize(forSize: Vector3(150, 150, 150)) == 0.5, "a 150 mm cube still has 0.5 mm cells")
    }

    @Test func aLargePartGetsCoarserCellsOnlyAsFarAsTheCellBudgetNeeds() {
        // A plate a metre square and 10 mm thick has 80 million 0.5 mm cells, so its cell grows a little; a metre cube's grows a lot.
        let middling = OccupancyGrid.cellSize(forSize: Vector3(300, 300, 300))
        #expect(middling > 0.5 && middling < 1.1, "\(middling)")
        let plate = OccupancyGrid.cellSize(forSize: Vector3(1_000, 1_000, 10))
        #expect(plate > 0.5 && plate < 1, "\(plate)")
        let cube = OccupancyGrid.cellSize(forSize: Vector3(1_000, 1_000, 1_000))
        #expect(cube > 2.5 && cube < 4, "\(cube)")
        for size in [Vector3(1_000, 1_000, 10), Vector3(1_000, 1_000, 1_000), Vector3(5_000, 20, 20)] {
            let grid = OccupancyGrid(bounds: BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: size))
            #expect(grid.counts.x * grid.counts.y * grid.counts.z <= OccupancyGrid.largestCellCount, "\(size)")
        }
        #expect(OccupancyGrid.cellSize(forSize: Vector3(0, 0, 0)) > 0, "a point has a cell")
    }

    @Test func aHoleIsSeenFromFourCellsAcrossWhichIsTwoMillimetresExceptInAHugePart() {
        #expect(OccupancyGrid.smallestSeenHole(forSize: Vector3(100, 80, 40)) == 2)
        #expect(OccupancyGrid.smallestSeenHole(forSize: Vector3(10, 10, 10)) == 4 * 10.0 / 32)
        #expect(OccupancyGrid.smallestSeenHole(forSize: Vector3(1_000, 1_000, 1_000)) > 2, "the limit of the grid, which the check says")
    }

    @Test func theGridHasTwoCellsOfPaddingAndIndexesRoundTrip() {
        let grid = OccupancyGrid(bounds: BoundingBox(min: Vector3(0, 0, 0), max: Vector3(10, 20, 30)))
        #expect(grid.cell == 0.5)
        #expect(grid.counts == SIMD3(24, 44, 64), "20, 40 and 60 cells and two of padding either side")
        let index = grid.index(3, 4, 5)
        #expect(grid.coordinates(of: index) == SIMD3(3, 4, 5))
        let centre = grid.center(of: index)
        #expect(grid.index(containing: centre) == index)
        #expect(grid.index(containing: Vector3(-100, 0, 0)) == nil && grid.index(containing: Vector3(.nan, 0, 0)) == nil)
    }

    /// A grid with a solid shell of one cell round a block of empty cells, and a second block in the open.
    func shell(openAt hole: Int? = nil) -> OccupancyGrid {
        var grid = OccupancyGrid(bounds: BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: Vector3(10, 10, 10)))
        for k in 4..<14 {
            for j in 4..<14 {
                for i in 4..<14 {
                    let onShell = [i, j, k].contains { $0 == 4 || $0 == 13 }
                    grid.setSolid(onShell, at: grid.index(i, j, k))
                }
            }
        }
        if let hole { grid.setSolid(false, at: hole) }
        return grid
    }

    @Test func anEmptyBlockInsideASolidShellIsAVoid() throws {
        let grid = shell()
        let found = try EnclosedVoids(grid)
        #expect(found.voids.count == 1 && found.voids[0].count == 8 * 8 * 8)
        #expect(found.label[grid.index(8, 8, 8)] == 0 && found.label[grid.index(0, 0, 0)] == -1)
    }

    @Test func aHoleInTheShellLetsTheOutsideIn() throws {
        #expect(try EnclosedVoids(shell(openAt: shell().index(8, 8, 13))).voids.isEmpty)
    }

    @Test func aSingleEmptyCellIsNotAVoid() throws {
        var grid = OccupancyGrid(bounds: BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: Vector3(10, 10, 10)))
        for index in 0..<grid.cellCount { grid.setSolid(true, at: index) }
        grid.setSolid(false, at: grid.index(8, 8, 8))
        grid.setSolid(false, at: grid.index(0, 0, 0))
        #expect(try EnclosedVoids(grid).voids.isEmpty, "one cell of air is below what the grid can see")
        grid.setSolid(false, at: grid.index(9, 8, 8))
        #expect(try EnclosedVoids(grid).voids.count == 1)
    }

    @Test func twoPocketsAreTwoVoids() throws {
        var grid = OccupancyGrid(bounds: BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: Vector3(10, 10, 10)))
        for index in 0..<grid.cellCount {
            let c = grid.coordinates(of: index)
            grid.setSolid((4..<30).contains(c.x) && (4..<30).contains(c.y) && (4..<30).contains(c.z), at: index)
        }
        for (i, j) in [(8, 8), (20, 20)] {
            for dx in 0..<3 { grid.setSolid(false, at: grid.index(i + dx, j, j)) }
        }
        let found = try EnclosedVoids(grid)
        #expect(found.voids.count == 2 && found.voids.allSatisfy { $0.count == 3 })
    }

    @Test func fillingFromADistanceMarksTheCellsNearTheSurfaceToo() throws {
        var grid = OccupancyGrid(bounds: BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: Vector3(10, 10, 10)))
        // A ball of radius 3 about (5, 5, 5).
        try grid.fill { points in points.map { ($0 - Vector3(5, 5, 5)).length - 3 } }
        #expect(grid.isSolid[grid.index(containing: Vector3(5, 5, 5)) ?? 0])
        #expect(grid.isSolid[grid.index(containing: Vector3(5 + 3.1, 5, 5)) ?? 0], "just outside, within half a cell")
        #expect(!grid.isSolid[grid.index(containing: Vector3(5 + 4.2, 5, 5)) ?? 0])
    }

    /// The cells a signed distance makes solid, one at a time: what `fill` must agree with.
    func bruteForce(_ grid: OccupancyGrid, _ distance: (Vector3) -> Double) -> [Bool] {
        (0..<grid.cellCount).map { distance(grid.center(of: $0)) <= grid.reach }
    }

    @Test func fillingByBlocksGivesWhatFillingCellByCellGives() throws {
        let centre = Vector3(10, 10, 10)
        let ball = { (point: Vector3) in (point - centre).length - 6 }
        var grid = OccupancyGrid(bounds: BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: Vector3(20, 20, 20)))
        var asked = 0
        try grid.fill { points in
            asked += points.count
            return points.map(ball)
        }
        #expect(grid.isSolid == bruteForce(grid, ball))
        #expect(asked < grid.cellCount / 2, "the surface is asked about cell by cell, the rest by blocks: \(asked) of \(grid.cellCount)")
    }

    @Test func aDistanceThatGrowsFasterThanOneMillimetreAMillimetreStillFillsExactlyWithItsBound() throws {
        let ball = { (point: Vector3) in 2.5 * ((point - Vector3(10, 10, 10)).length - 6) }
        var grid = OccupancyGrid(bounds: BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: Vector3(20, 20, 20)))
        try grid.fill(lipschitz: 2.5) { points in points.map(ball) }
        #expect(grid.isSolid == bruteForce(grid, ball))
    }

    @Test func aFillCanBeWaitedForAndGivesTheSameCells() async throws {
        let ball = { (point: Vector3) in (point - Vector3(10, 10, 10)).length - 6 }
        var waited = OccupancyGrid(bounds: BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: Vector3(20, 20, 20)))
        try await waited.fill(awaiting: { points in points.map(ball) })
        var direct = OccupancyGrid(bounds: BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: Vector3(20, 20, 20)))
        try direct.fill { points in points.map(ball) }
        #expect(waited.isSolid == direct.isSolid)
    }

    @Test func aDrainHoleOfTwoMillimetresIsSeenThroughAtTheDefaultCell() throws {
        // A plate 6 mm thick with a cavity 3 mm high, and a hole of the default 2 mm drilled up through its floor into the middle of the cavity: the
        // grid must see the hole, or Check Trapped Volumes would go on reporting a void that drains. (Each is a max or a min of
        // distances that grow at most one for one, so the whole is a valid bound for `fill`.)
        let middling = OccupancyGrid.cellSize(forSize: Vector3(300, 300, 300))
        #expect(middling > 0.5 && middling < 1.1, "\(middling)")
        let plate = BoundingBox(min: Vector3(-20, -20, 0), max: Vector3(20, 20, 6))
        func block(_ p: Vector3) -> Double { max(abs(p.x) - 20, abs(p.y) - 20, abs(p.z - 3) - 3) }
        func cavity(_ p: Vector3) -> Double { max(abs(p.x) - 17, abs(p.y) - 17, abs(p.z - 3) - 1.5) }
        func hole(_ p: Vector3) -> Double { max((p.x * p.x + p.y * p.y).squareRoot() - 1, p.z - 3) }
        var drained = OccupancyGrid(bounds: plate)
        try drained.fill { points in points.map { max(block($0), -min(cavity($0), hole($0))) } }
        #expect(try EnclosedVoids(drained).voids.isEmpty, "the hole opens the cavity to the outside")
        var sealed = OccupancyGrid(bounds: plate)
        try sealed.fill { points in points.map { max(block($0), -cavity($0)) } }
        #expect(try EnclosedVoids(sealed).voids.count == 1, "and without it the cavity is a void")
    }
}
```

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

/// Check Trapped Volumes (implicit spec §4.6, §7): a sealed internal void is found and its walls highlighted.
struct TrappedVolumeCheckTests {
    @Test func aSealedVoidIsFoundAndItsWallsAreTheOnesThatLookIntoIt() throws {
        let hollow = MeshFixtures.hollowCube(size: 10, wall: 1, divisions: 8)
        let outerCount = 6 * 8 * 8 * 2
        let result = try TrappedVolumeCheck.run(hollow)
        #expect(result.voidCount == 1)
        #expect(result.triangles.count == hollow.triangleCount - outerCount, "the void's own faces, and no others")
        #expect(result.triangles.allSatisfy { $0 >= outerCount })
        #expect(result.cell == 10.0 / 32, "the grid's cell is reported for the node's fact")
    }

    @Test func twoVoidsAreCounted() throws {
        let part = MeshFixtures.cubeWithVoids(size: 20, divisions: 4, voids: [SIMD4(2, 2, 2, 5), SIMD4(11, 11, 11, 6)])
        #expect(try TrappedVolumeCheck.run(part).voidCount == 2)
    }

    @Test func aSolidPartHasNoVoids() throws {
        #expect(try TrappedVolumeCheck.run(MeshFixtures.subdividedCube(divisions: 4)).voidCount == 0)
        #expect(try TrappedVolumeCheck.run(MeshFixtures.sphereMesh(radius: 5, subdivisions: 3)).voidCount == 0)
    }

    @Test func aVoidTooSmallForTheGridIsNotFound() throws {
        let part = MeshFixtures.cubeWithVoids(size: 10, divisions: 4, voids: [SIMD4(4.9, 4.9, 4.9, 0.2)])
        #expect(try TrappedVolumeCheck.run(part).voidCount == 0)
    }

    @Test func aVoidInsideAThinWallIsStillSealed() throws {
        // The wall is 0.2 mm and the cells are 0.31 mm: the wall must not be a gap in the grid.
        #expect(try TrappedVolumeCheck.run(MeshFixtures.hollowCube(size: 10, wall: 0.2, divisions: 10)).voidCount == 1)
    }

    @Test func aMeshThatIsNotClosedHasNoInside() {
        let open = MeshFixtures.patch(divisions: 4)
        #expect(throws: MeshDistanceError.notClosed) { try TrappedVolumeCheck.run(open) }
    }

    @Test func aCavityInsideAMeshedBallIsFound() async throws {
        let ball = ImplicitSolid.subtract([.sphere(radius: 3), .sphere(radius: 1.5)], blend: .constant(0))
        let mesh = try await ImplicitMesher(evaluator: CPUBatchEvaluator(ball)).mesh(ball, resolution: 0.2, quality: .export)
        #expect(MeshTopology(mesh).eulerCharacteristic == 4, "two closed shells")
        let result = try TrappedVolumeCheck.run(mesh)
        #expect(result.voidCount == 1 && !result.triangles.isEmpty)
        let flagged = Set(result.triangles)
        for index in flagged { #expect(mesh.corners(of: index).0.length < 1.7, "only the cavity's wall is highlighted") }
    }

    @Test func anEmptyMeshHasNoVoids() throws {
        #expect(try TrappedVolumeCheck.run(TriangleMesh(positions: [], triangles: [])).voidCount == 0)
    }
}
```

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel
import Testing
@testable import CreatorNodes

/// Check Trapped Volumes (implicit spec §3.6): the highlight, the exact warning and the label on a preview.
struct CheckTrappedVolumesNodeTests {
    /// A ball with a sealed cavity, meshed by the Mesh node, then checked.
    func checked(_ h: inout Harness) -> (mesh: Node, check: Node) {
        // Radii 3 and 1.5 as a list: the ball, then the cavity cut from it.
        let radii = h.add(SeriesNode.self, ["start": .number(3), "step": .number(-1.5), "count": .integer(2)])
        let spheres = h.add(ImplicitSphereNode.self)
        let subtract = h.add(ImplicitSubtractNode.self)
        let mesh = h.add(MeshNode.self, ["export resolution": .number(0.2), "preview resolution": .number(0.2)])
        let check = h.add(CheckTrappedVolumesNode.self)
        h.wire(radii, "values", to: spheres, "radius")
        h.wire(spheres, "implicit", to: subtract, "shapes")
        h.wire(subtract, "implicit", to: mesh, "implicit")
        h.wire(mesh, "mesh", to: check, "mesh")
        return (mesh, check)
    }

    @Test func aSealedCavityIsHighlightedAndWarnedAboutInTheSpecsWords() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let (mesh, check) = checked(&h)
        let report = try await h.runForExport([check])
        let made = try #require(report.triangleMesh(check)), source = try #require(report.triangleMesh(mesh))
        let highlight = try #require(made.highlights.first)
        #expect(made.highlights.count == 1 && !highlight.triangles.isEmpty && highlight.label == "Enclosed voids")
        #expect(made.positions == source.positions && made.id != source.id)
        #expect(report.warning(check) == "1 enclosed void can't drain.")
        #expect(made.inspectorFacts.contains(PayloadFact("Enclosed voids", "1")))
    }

    @Test func aPreviewSaysItIsApproximate() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let (_, check) = checked(&h)
        let message = try #require(try await h.run([check]).warning(check))
        #expect(message == "1 enclosed void can't drain (approximate: preview resolution).", "\(message)")
    }

    @Test func aSolidBallHasNothingToReport() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let sphere = h.add(ImplicitSphereNode.self, ["radius": .number(3)])
        let mesh = h.add(MeshNode.self, ["preview resolution": .number(0.3)])
        let check = h.add(CheckTrappedVolumesNode.self)
        h.wire(sphere, "implicit", to: mesh, "implicit")
        h.wire(mesh, "mesh", to: check, "mesh")
        let report = try await h.run([check])
        let made = try #require(report.triangleMesh(check))
        #expect(made.highlights.isEmpty && report.isOK(check))
        #expect(made.inspectorFacts.contains(PayloadFact("Enclosed voids", "0")))
        #expect(made.inspectorFacts.contains { $0.label == "Grid cell" && $0.value.hasSuffix(" mm") })
        #expect(report.warning(check) == nil, "a part this size has the usual cells")
    }

    @Test func aHugePartSaysItsCellsAreCoarseAndAUsualOneSaysNothing() {
        #expect(CheckWording.coarseGrid(cell: 0.5).isEmpty && CheckWording.coarseGrid(cell: 0.2).isEmpty)
        #expect(CheckWording.coarseGrid(cell: 3.1)
            == ["The part is large: the check's cells are 3.1 mm, so holes under 12.4 mm may not count as open."])
    }

    @Test func thePluralsAreTheSpecsWords() {
        #expect(CheckWording.voids(3, label: nil) == "3 enclosed voids can't drain.")
        #expect(CheckWording.voids(1, label: nil) == "1 enclosed void can't drain.")
        #expect(CheckWording.voids(2, label: "approximate: preview resolution")
            == "2 enclosed voids can't drain (approximate: preview resolution).")
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Checks/OccupancyGrid.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGeometry

/// A coarse grid of cells over a part (implicit spec §4.6): which cells are solid, in the sense that matters for draining. A cell is
/// solid when its centre is inside the part or within half a cell of the surface, so a wall thinner than a cell is never a gap in the
/// grid, and a void or a channel has to be larger than about a cell to be seen. The grid has two empty cells of padding round the part, so
/// its corner is always outside.
public struct OccupancyGrid: Sendable {
    /// The most cells a grid may have, padding included. The flood fill holds about ten bytes a cell, so this is some 300 MB. A part that
    /// would need more gets coarser cells (`cellSize(forSize:)`): 0.5 mm holds up to a 150 mm cube; a 300 mm cube gets 1 mm cells and a
    /// metre cube 3 mm.
    public static let largestCellCount = 32_000_000

    /// The largest edge of a cell, in millimetres. The spec says 2 mm; a drain hole of the default 2 mm is then one cell across and
    /// the grid cannot see through it (the cell beside its wall is "within half a cell of the surface"), so the Check Trapped Volumes
    /// that follows Drain Holes would still report the void. A hole is seen when it is about four cells across: 0.5 mm.
    public static let largestCell = 0.5

    /// The smallest hole the grid of a part `size` across reliably sees through, four cells across: 2 mm for most parts.
    public static func smallestSeenHole(forSize size: Vector3) -> Double {
        4 * cellSize(forSize: size)
    }

    /// The edge of a cell for a part `size` across: 0.5 mm or 1/32 of the longest side, whichever is finer, made coarser only as far as
    /// keeps the grid within `largestCellCount` cells. So a part of up to about 150 mm in each direction gets 0.5 mm cells, and a larger
    /// one gets cells in which a 2 mm hole is no longer seen (the check and Drain Holes say so).
    public static func cellSize(forSize size: Vector3) -> Double {
        let longest = max(size.x, size.y, size.z, 1e-6)
        var cell = min(largestCell, longest / 32)
        func cells(_ edge: Double) -> Double {
            [size.x, size.y, size.z].reduce(1) { $0 * ((max($1, 0) / edge).rounded(.up) + 4) }
        }
        while cells(cell) > Double(largestCellCount) { cell *= 1.02 }
        return cell
    }

    /// Cells along a side of the blocks `fill` classifies together.
    static let blockSide = 8
    /// The most cell centres asked for at once.
    static let batch = 1 << 18

    public let origin: Vector3
    public let cell: Double
    public let counts: SIMD3<Int>
    public private(set) var isSolid: [Bool]

    /// The cells for a part inside `bounds`, all empty.
    public init(bounds: BoundingBox) {
        let size = bounds.size
        cell = Self.cellSize(forSize: size)
        origin = bounds.min - Vector3(2 * cell, 2 * cell, 2 * cell)
        counts = SIMD3(Int((size.x / cell).rounded(.up)) + 4, Int((size.y / cell).rounded(.up)) + 4, Int((size.z / cell).rounded(.up)) + 4)
        isSolid = [Bool](repeating: false, count: counts.x * counts.y * counts.z)
    }

    public var cellCount: Int { isSolid.count }

    /// The flat number of cell (i, j, k), x fastest.
    public func index(_ i: Int, _ j: Int, _ k: Int) -> Int { (k * counts.y + j) * counts.x + i }

    /// The cell numbers (i, j, k) of a flat index.
    public func coordinates(of index: Int) -> SIMD3<Int> {
        SIMD3(index % counts.x, (index / counts.x) % counts.y, index / (counts.x * counts.y))
    }

    /// The centre of the cell with this flat index.
    public func center(of index: Int) -> Vector3 {
        let c = coordinates(of: index)
        return origin + Vector3(Double(c.x) + 0.5, Double(c.y) + 0.5, Double(c.z) + 0.5) * cell
    }

    /// The flat index of the cell that holds `point`, or `nil` outside the grid.
    public func index(containing point: Vector3) -> Int? {
        let offset = (point - origin) * (1 / cell)
        guard offset.x.isFinite, offset.y.isFinite, offset.z.isFinite, offset.x >= 0, offset.y >= 0, offset.z >= 0 else { return nil }
        let (i, j, k) = (Int(offset.x), Int(offset.y), Int(offset.z))
        guard i < counts.x, j < counts.y, k < counts.z else { return nil }
        return index(i, j, k)
    }

    /// Sets whether the cell with this flat index is solid.
    public mutating func setSolid(_ solid: Bool, at index: Int) {
        isSolid[index] = solid
    }

    // MARK: Filling from a distance

    /// A run of cells, `low` to `high` (exclusive), classified together.
    struct Block {
        var low: SIMD3<Int>
        var high: SIMD3<Int>
    }

    /// What a block's centre value says about its cells: all solid, all empty, or each cell must be asked.
    enum Verdict {
        case solid, empty, undecided
    }

    /// The distance at which a cell is solid: half a cell.
    var reach: Double { cell / 2 * 1.0001 }

    func blocks() -> [Block] {
        var found: [Block] = []
        let side = Self.blockSide
        for k in stride(from: 0, to: counts.z, by: side) {
            for j in stride(from: 0, to: counts.y, by: side) {
                for i in stride(from: 0, to: counts.x, by: side) {
                    found.append(Block(low: SIMD3(i, j, k), high: SIMD3(min(i + side, counts.x), min(j + side, counts.y),
                                                                          min(k + side, counts.z))))
                }
            }
        }
        return found
    }

    /// The point in the middle of a block, and the distance from it to the farthest cell centre in it.
    func middle(of block: Block) -> (point: Vector3, radius: Double) {
        let low = origin + Vector3(Double(block.low.x), Double(block.low.y), Double(block.low.z)) * cell
        let high = origin + Vector3(Double(block.high.x), Double(block.high.y), Double(block.high.z)) * cell
        return ((low + high) * 0.5, (high - low).length / 2)
    }

    /// The signed distance is `lipschitz`-Lipschitz, so from `value` at a block's middle every cell centre in it is within `lipschitz × radius` of it.
    func verdict(value: Double, radius: Double, lipschitz: Double) -> Verdict {
        if value + lipschitz * radius <= reach { return .solid }
        if value - lipschitz * radius > reach { return .empty }
        return .undecided
    }

    mutating func mark(_ block: Block) {
        for k in block.low.z..<block.high.z {
            for j in block.low.y..<block.high.y {
                for i in block.low.x..<block.high.x { isSolid[index(i, j, k)] = true }
            }
        }
    }

    /// The flat indices and centres of the cells of `blocks`, in order.
    func cells(of blocks: ArraySlice<Block>) -> (indices: [Int], points: [Vector3]) {
        var indices: [Int] = []
        for block in blocks {
            for k in block.low.z..<block.high.z {
                for j in block.low.y..<block.high.y {
                    for i in block.low.x..<block.high.x { indices.append(index(i, j, k)) }
                }
            }
        }
        return (indices, indices.map { center(of: $0) })
    }

    /// The runs of undecided blocks that hold about `batch` cells each.
    func batches(of blocks: [Block]) -> [ArraySlice<Block>] {
        let perBatch = max(1, Self.batch / (Self.blockSide * Self.blockSide * Self.blockSide))
        return stride(from: 0, to: blocks.count, by: perBatch).map { blocks[$0..<min($0 + perBatch, blocks.count)] }
    }

    /// Fills the grid from a signed distance (negative inside, `lipschitz`-Lipschitz): a cell is solid when its centre is within half a
    /// cell of the surface or inside it. Blocks of 8 × 8 × 8 cells are first classified by the distance at their middle, and only the
    /// blocks the surface may cross are asked cell by cell, so the work follows the surface and not the volume. `distances` gives the
    /// distance at each of the points it is asked for, in order.
    public mutating func fill(lipschitz: Double = 1, from distances: ([Vector3]) throws -> [Double]) throws {
        let all = blocks()
        let middles = all.map { middle(of: $0) }
        var values: [Double] = []
        for start in stride(from: 0, to: middles.count, by: Self.batch) {
            try Task.checkCancellation()
            values += try distances(middles[start..<min(start + Self.batch, middles.count)].map(\.point))
        }
        var undecided: [Block] = []
        for (index, block) in all.enumerated() {
            switch verdict(value: values[index], radius: middles[index].radius, lipschitz: max(lipschitz, 1)) {
            case .solid: mark(block)
            case .empty: break
            case .undecided: undecided.append(block)
            }
        }
        for batch in batches(of: undecided) {
            try Task.checkCancellation()
            let (indices, points) = cells(of: batch)
            let found = try distances(points)
            for (offset, index) in indices.enumerated() { isSolid[index] = found[offset] <= reach }
        }
    }

    /// `fill(lipschitz:from:)` for a distance that has to be waited for (the GPU).
    public mutating func fill(lipschitz: Double = 1, awaiting distances: ([Vector3]) async throws -> [Double]) async throws {
        let all = blocks()
        let middles = all.map { middle(of: $0) }
        var values: [Double] = []
        for start in stride(from: 0, to: middles.count, by: Self.batch) {
            try Task.checkCancellation()
            values += try await distances(middles[start..<min(start + Self.batch, middles.count)].map(\.point))
        }
        var undecided: [Block] = []
        for (index, block) in all.enumerated() {
            switch verdict(value: values[index], radius: middles[index].radius, lipschitz: max(lipschitz, 1)) {
            case .solid: mark(block)
            case .empty: break
            case .undecided: undecided.append(block)
            }
        }
        for batch in batches(of: undecided) {
            try Task.checkCancellation()
            let (indices, points) = cells(of: batch)
            let found = try await distances(points)
            for (offset, index) in indices.enumerated() { isSolid[index] = found[offset] <= reach }
        }
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Checks/EnclosedVoids.swift`:

```swift
/// The empty cells of an `OccupancyGrid` that the outside cannot reach (implicit spec §4.6): flood fill from the corner through
/// empty cells that share a face; whatever empty is left over is a pocket, and the pockets of more than one cell are voids.
public struct EnclosedVoids: Sendable {
    /// The void each cell belongs to, or -1.
    public let label: [Int32]
    /// The flat cell indices of each void, in order of their lowest flat index.
    public let voids: [[Int32]]

    public init(_ grid: OccupancyGrid) throws {
        let counts = grid.counts
        let strides = [1, counts.x, counts.x * counts.y]
        var reached = grid.isSolid
        func fill(from start: Int, collecting: inout [Int32]) throws {
            var stack = [Int32(start)]
            reached[start] = true
            while let next = stack.popLast() {
                if collecting.count & 0xFFFF == 0 { try Task.checkCancellation() }
                collecting.append(next)
                let c = grid.coordinates(of: Int(next))
                for axis in 0..<3 {
                    for step in [-1, 1] where c[axis] + step >= 0 && c[axis] + step < counts[axis] {
                        let neighbour = Int(next) + step * strides[axis]
                        if !reached[neighbour] {
                            reached[neighbour] = true
                            stack.append(Int32(neighbour))
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        // The corner is padding, so empty: everything the fill reaches is outside.
        var outside: [Int32] = []
        if !reached[0] { try fill(from: 0, collecting: &outside) }
        var label = [Int32](repeating: -1, count: grid.cellCount)
        var voids: [[Int32]] = []
        for start in 0..<grid.cellCount where !reached[start] {
            var cells: [Int32] = []
            try fill(from: start, collecting: &cells)
            // A pocket of a single cell is below what the grid can tell from the surface.
            guard cells.count > 1 else { continue }
            for cell in cells { label[Int(cell)] = Int32(voids.count) }
            voids.append(cells)
        }
        self.label = label
        self.voids = voids
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Checks/TrappedVolumeCheck.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGeometry

/// Trapped volumes (implicit spec §4.6): a coarse occupancy grid of the mesh (0.5 mm cells, or 1/32 of the longest side if that is
/// finer), flood filled from outside. Empty cells the fill cannot reach, in pockets of more than one cell, are voids that cannot
/// drain. A triangle looks into a void when a void cell lies a little way from it along its normal, and is highlighted.
public enum TrappedVolumeCheck {
    public struct Result: Sendable {
        /// The number of enclosed voids.
        public var voidCount: Int
        /// The triangles that face into a void, in order.
        public var triangles: [Int]
        /// The edge of the grid's cells in millimetres, 0 for a mesh with no triangles. A hole or a void must be a few cells across to be
        /// seen, so a cell larger than `OccupancyGrid.largestCell` (a very large part) means smaller drain holes may not count.
        public var cell: Double = 0
    }

    /// - Throws: `MeshDistanceError.notClosed` when the mesh has no inside, and `CancellationError`.
    public static func run(_ mesh: TriangleMesh) throws -> Result {
        guard let bounds = mesh.bounds, mesh.triangleCount > 0 else { return Result(voidCount: 0, triangles: []) }
        let positions = (0..<mesh.vertexCount).map { mesh.point($0) }
        let distances = try SignedDistanceMesh(positions: positions, indices: mesh.triangles)
        var grid = OccupancyGrid(bounds: bounds)
        try grid.fill { points in points.map { distances.signedDistance(at: $0) } }
        let found = try EnclosedVoids(grid)
        guard !found.voids.isEmpty else { return Result(voidCount: 0, triangles: [], cell: grid.cell) }
        var facing: [Int] = []
        for index in 0..<mesh.triangleCount {
            if index & 0xFFF == 0 { try Task.checkCancellation() }
            let (a, b, c) = mesh.corners(of: index)
            guard let normal = (b - a).cross(c - a).normalized else { continue }
            let centre = (a + b + c) * (1.0 / 3)
            // The cells next to the surface count as solid, so look a cell or two out.
            let looksIn = [0.75, 1.25, 1.75].contains { reach in
                grid.index(containing: centre + normal * (reach * grid.cell)).map { found.label[$0] >= 0 } ?? false
            }
            if looksIn { facing.append(index) }
        }
        return Result(voidCount: found.voids.count, triangles: facing, cell: grid.cell)
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorNodes/Implicit/MeshTools/CheckTrappedVolumesNode.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation

/// Finds the pockets a part encloses (implicit spec §3.6, §4.6): empty space the outside cannot reach, which powder, resin or support
/// would be trapped in. The mesh comes out as it went in, the walls around each void highlighted, and a warning counts them. On a
/// preview mesh the warning says it is approximate.
public enum CheckTrappedVolumesNode: NodeDefinition {
    public static let typeID = "creator.checkTrappedVolumes"
    public static let displayName = "Check Trapped Volumes"
    public static let category = NodeCategory.mesh
    public static let inputs = [SocketSpec("mesh", .mesh)]
    public static let outputs = [SocketSpec("mesh", .mesh)]
    public static let inspector = [
        InspectorSection(title: "Check Trapped Volumes", controls: [
            .note("Enclosed voids are highlighted. Drain Holes can drill them out."),
        ]),
    ]

    public static func evaluate(_ inputs: NodeInputs, kernel: any Kernel, context: EvalContext) async throws -> NodeOutputs {
        let (mesh, warnings) = try await MeshInput.mesh(try inputs.scalar("mesh"), socket: "mesh", context: context)
        let result: TrappedVolumeCheck.Result
        do {
            result = try TrappedVolumeCheck.run(mesh)
        } catch let error as MeshDistanceError {
            throw NodeError.invalidValue(error.userMessage.replacing("This solid", with: "This mesh"))
        }
        let count = result.voidCount.formatted(.number.locale(.messages))
        let facts = [PayloadFact("Enclosed voids", count), PayloadFact("Grid cell", result.cell.millimetreText(locale: .messages) + " mm")]
        let coarse = CheckWording.coarseGrid(cell: result.cell)
        guard result.voidCount > 0 else {
            return NodeOutputs(["mesh": .mesh(mesh.checked(highlights: [], facts: facts))], warnings: warnings + coarse)
        }
        let highlight = MeshHighlight(triangles: result.triangles, label: "Enclosed voids")
        let flagged = mesh.checked(highlights: [highlight], facts: facts)
        let text = CheckWording.voids(result.voidCount, label: CheckWording.label(for: mesh))
        return NodeOutputs(["mesh": .mesh(flagged)], warnings: warnings + [text] + coarse)
    }
}
```

```swift
    /// The warning of a part so large that the grid's cells are coarser than 0.5 mm: a drain hole of the default 2 mm is then smaller
    /// than the cells can see through, so the check may report a void that is drained. Empty for the usual cell
    /// (`OccupancyGrid.largestCell`).
    static func coarseGrid(cell: Double) -> [String] {
        guard cell > OccupancyGrid.largestCell else { return [] }
        let (edge, hole) = (cell.millimetreText(locale: .messages), (4 * cell).millimetreText(locale: .messages))
        return ["The part is large: the check's cells are \(edge) mm, so holes under \(hole) mm may not count as open."]
    }

    /// `text`, then the label in brackets when there is one, then the full stop.
```

`swift test --filter "OccupancyGridTests|TrappedVolumeCheckTests|CheckTrappedVolumesNodeTests|OutputNodeTests|ImplicitLibraryTests"` and `swiftlint lint --strict`.
Expected: the new suites hold 26 tests, all pass (the meshed-ball tests take 10 to 40 s in a debug build); lint prints nothing.

```bash
git add Sources Tests
git commit -m "feat(nodes): Check Trapped Volumes, by a coarse grid flood filled from outside"
```

### Task 5: Drain Holes

Spec §3.6: `implicit`; `diameter` (mm, default 2); `direction` (vector, default −Z); `mode`: At Points (a `points` list) / Automatic → *implicit
minus a cylinder per hole. Automatic finds trapped voids on a coarse grid (§4.6) and drills from each void's lowest point along `direction` to the
outside.*

- **No new `ImplicitSolid` case.** A hole is `.moved(.cylinder(radius, halfHeight), frame)` and the result `.subtract([solid] + holes)`, both
  existing cases with exact distances and lipschitz 1, so the CPU evaluator, the Metal compiler and 7c-2's new cases need no change and the
  result meshes on the GPU for free.
- **Automatic runs the trapped-volume test on the implicit.** `OccupancyGrid.of(solid, using: evaluator)` evaluates the implicit at the cell centres
  (solid where the value is at most half a cell, Task 4), then `EnclosedVoids`. It uses the node's own evaluator selection (Metal when the
  tree compiles), 262,144 centres at a time, with cancellation checked between.
- **Where the hole starts.** The cell centre farthest along `direction` (the lowest, for −Z); where several tie in that layer, the one nearest
  their middle. The hole runs from there along `direction` for the solid's bounding diagonal plus the diameter, so it always leaves the part.
- **At Points** drills from each wired point the same way (the point is where the hole starts, so a point in the material is a blind hole's
  bottom); with no points it says "Connect points to “points”, or choose Automatic." Mode defaults to **Automatic** (see User decisions).
- With no void, Automatic returns its input unchanged and says nothing.
- **A hole the check cannot see through warns.** Check Trapped Volumes sees a hole about four of its cells across (Task 4: 2 mm for a part up to 150 mm, more for a larger one), so a
  `diameter` under that warns "A hole of 1 mm is smaller than Check Trapped Volumes can see through (2 mm for this part), so it may still report the void." (`CheckWording.smallHole`;
  `aHoleTooSmallForTheCheckToSeeWarnsAndTheDefaultDoesNot`). The hole is still drilled.

- Modify: `BuiltInNodes.swift`, `ImplicitLibraryTests.swift`, `OutputNodeTests.swift` (81; implicit 11)

**Interfaces:**

- Produces: `OccupancyGrid.of(_:using:) async throws`; `DrainHoles.drainPoints(of:direction:using:)`, `DrainHoles.drilled(_:at:diameter:direction:)`;
  `DrainHolesNode` (`creator.drainHoles`; inputs `implicit`, `diameter`, `direction`, `mode`, `points`).

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

/// Drain holes (implicit spec §3.6, §4.6, §7): the voids of an implicit are found on its coarse grid and drilled out.
struct DrainHolesTests {
    /// A ball of radius 3 with a sealed cavity of radius 1.5 about the origin.
    let ball = ImplicitSolid.subtract([.sphere(radius: 3), .sphere(radius: 1.5)], blend: .constant(0))

    @Test func theCavityOfABallIsFoundAtItsLowestPoint() async throws {
        let points = try await DrainHoles.drainPoints(of: ball, direction: Vector3(0, 0, -1), using: CPUBatchEvaluator(ball))
        let point = try #require(points.first)
        #expect(points.count == 1)
        #expect(point.z < -1.1 && point.z > -1.6 && abs(point.x) < 0.2 && abs(point.y) < 0.2, "\(point)")
        let sideways = try await DrainHoles.drainPoints(of: ball, direction: .unitX, using: CPUBatchEvaluator(ball))
        #expect(try #require(sideways.first).x > 1.1, "the lowest along +X is the far side")
    }

    @Test func aSolidBallHasNothingToDrainAndTwoCavitiesNeedTwoHoles() async throws {
        let solid = ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 3)
        #expect(try await DrainHoles.drainPoints(of: solid, direction: .unitZ, using: CPUBatchEvaluator(solid)).isEmpty)
        let slab = ImplicitSolid.box(halfSize: Vector3(8, 3, 3), cornerRadius: 0)
        func cavity(at x: Double) -> ImplicitSolid {
            .moved(.sphere(radius: 1.2), RigidMove(origin: Vector3(x, 0, 0), xAxis: .unitX, yAxis: .unitY, zAxis: .unitZ))
        }
        let (left, right) = (cavity(at: -4), cavity(at: 4))
        let two = ImplicitSolid.subtract([slab, left, right], blend: .constant(0))
        let points = try await DrainHoles.drainPoints(of: two, direction: Vector3(0, 0, -1), using: CPUBatchEvaluator(two))
        #expect(points.count == 2 && points.map(\.x).sorted()[0] < 0 && points.map(\.x).sorted()[1] > 0)
    }

    @Test func aHoleLeavesAnOpenTunnelFromThePointToTheOutside() {
        let drilled = DrainHoles.drilled(ball, at: [Vector3(0, 0, -1.4)], diameter: 1, direction: Vector3(0, 0, -1))
        #expect(ball.signedDistance(at: Vector3(0, 0, -2.5)) < 0 && drilled.signedDistance(at: Vector3(0, 0, -2.5)) > 0)
        #expect(drilled.signedDistance(at: Vector3(0.4, 0, -2.5)) > 0, "inside the hole's radius")
        #expect(drilled.signedDistance(at: Vector3(0.9, 0, -2.5)) < 0, "outside it the wall is still there")
        #expect(drilled.signedDistance(at: Vector3(0, 0, 2.5)) < 0, "nothing is drilled the other way")
        #expect(drilled.signedDistance(at: Vector3(0, 0, -3.5)) > 0)
        #expect(drilled.bounds == ball.bounds)
    }

    @Test func aHoleAlongAnyDirectionPointsThatWay() {
        for direction in [Vector3(1, 0, 0), Vector3(0, 1, 0), Vector3(1, 1, 1).normalized ?? .unitX, Vector3(0, 0.0, 1)] {
            let drilled = DrainHoles.drilled(ball, at: [.zero], diameter: 0.8, direction: direction)
            #expect(drilled.signedDistance(at: direction * 2.2) > 0, "\(direction)")
            #expect(drilled.signedDistance(at: direction * -2.2) < 0, "\(direction)")
        }
    }

    @Test func noPointsOrNoDirectionLeavesTheSolidAlone() {
        #expect(DrainHoles.drilled(ball, at: [], diameter: 1, direction: .unitZ) == ball)
        #expect(DrainHoles.drilled(ball, at: [.zero], diameter: 1, direction: .zero) == ball)
    }

    @Test func aSolidWithNoFiniteBoundsHasNoGrid() async {
        let unbounded = ImplicitSolid.isosurface(.constant(0), level: 0, maxSlope: 1,
                                                 region: BoundingBox(min: Vector3(-.infinity, 0, 0), max: Vector3(1, 1, 1)))
        await #expect(throws: ImplicitMeshingError.empty) {
            try await DrainHoles.drainPoints(of: unbounded, direction: .unitZ, using: CPUBatchEvaluator(unbounded))
        }
    }
}
```

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel
import Testing
@testable import CreatorNodes

/// Drain Holes (implicit spec §3.6): after the node, the void count is 0.
struct DrainHolesNodeTests {
    /// A ball of radius 3 with a sealed cavity of radius 1.5, as an implicit solid.
    func ball(_ h: inout Harness) -> Node {
        let radii = h.add(SeriesNode.self, ["start": .number(3), "step": .number(-1.5), "count": .integer(2)])
        let spheres = h.add(ImplicitSphereNode.self)
        let subtract = h.add(ImplicitSubtractNode.self)
        h.wire(radii, "values", to: spheres, "radius")
        h.wire(spheres, "implicit", to: subtract, "shapes")
        return subtract
    }

    /// The part through Drain Holes (or not), meshed and checked for voids.
    func voids(after drain: Node?, ball: Node, _ h: inout Harness) -> Node {
        let mesh = h.add(MeshNode.self, ["export resolution": .number(0.2)])
        let check = h.add(CheckTrappedVolumesNode.self)
        h.wire(drain ?? ball, "implicit", to: mesh, "implicit")
        h.wire(mesh, "mesh", to: check, "mesh")
        return check
    }

    @Test func automaticDrillingLeavesNoEnclosedVoid() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let part = ball(&h)
        let before = voids(after: nil, ball: part, &h)
        let drain = h.add(DrainHolesNode.self, ["diameter": .number(0.8)])
        h.wire(part, "implicit", to: drain, "implicit")
        let after = voids(after: drain, ball: part, &h)
        let report = try await h.runForExport([before, after])
        #expect(report.warning(before) == "1 enclosed void can't drain.")
        #expect(report.isOK(after), "\(String(describing: report.state(after)))")
        let drilled = try #require(report.triangleMesh(after))
        let topology = MeshTopology(drilled)
        #expect(topology.isClosedManifold && topology.eulerCharacteristic == 2, "one shell: a ball with a dent")
        #expect(drilled.signedVolume < (try #require(report.triangleMesh(before))).signedVolume)
        #expect(report.isOK(drain))
    }

    @Test func atPointsDrillsFromEachPointAlongTheDirection() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let part = ball(&h)
        let point = h.add(VectorNode.self, ["x": .number(0), "y": .number(0), "z": .number(-1.2)])
        let drain = h.add(DrainHolesNode.self, ["mode": .integer(0), "diameter": .number(0.8)])
        h.wire(part, "implicit", to: drain, "implicit")
        h.wire(point, "vector", to: drain, "points")
        let report = try await h.run([drain])
        let drilled = try #require(report.implicitSolid(drain))
        #expect(drilled.distance(0, 0, -2.5) > 0 && drilled.distance(0, 0, 2.5) < 0)
    }

    @Test func aHoleTooSmallForTheCheckToSeeWarnsAndTheDefaultDoesNot() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let box = h.add(ImplicitBoxNode.self, ["size": .vector(Vector3(40, 40, 40))])
        let thin = h.add(DrainHolesNode.self, ["diameter": .number(1)])
        let usual = h.add(DrainHolesNode.self)
        h.wire(box, "implicit", to: thin, "implicit")
        h.wire(box, "implicit", to: usual, "implicit")
        let report = try await h.run([thin, usual])
        #expect(report.warning(thin) == "A hole of 1 mm is smaller than Check Trapped Volumes can see through (2 mm for this part), "
            + "so it may still report the void.")
        #expect(report.warning(usual) == nil)
        #expect(CheckWording.smallHole(diameter: 2, seen: 2).isEmpty)
    }

    @Test func aSolidWithNoVoidIsPassedThroughAsItIs() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let sphere = h.add(ImplicitSphereNode.self, ["radius": .number(3)])
        let drain = h.add(DrainHolesNode.self)
        h.wire(sphere, "implicit", to: drain, "implicit")
        let report = try await h.run([sphere, drain])
        #expect(report.implicitSolid(drain) == report.implicitSolid(sphere) && report.isOK(drain))
    }

    @Test func badSettingsAreRefusedInPlainWords() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let part = ball(&h)
        let noPoints = h.add(DrainHolesNode.self, ["mode": .integer(0)])
        let noDirection = h.add(DrainHolesNode.self, ["direction": .vector(.zero)])
        let thin = h.add(DrainHolesNode.self, ["diameter": .number(0.01)])
        for node in [noPoints, noDirection, thin] { h.wire(part, "implicit", to: node, "implicit") }
        let report = try await h.run([noPoints, noDirection, thin])
        #expect(report.error(noPoints) == "Connect points to “points”, or choose Automatic.")
        #expect(report.error(noDirection) == "“direction” can't be zero.")
        #expect(report.error(thin) == "“diameter” must be at least 0.1 mm.")
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Checks/OccupancyGrid+Implicit.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGeometry

extension OccupancyGrid {
    /// The grid of an implicit solid, evaluated by `evaluator`: solid where the value is at most half a cell (the surface is that near,
    /// or the point is inside). Drain Holes runs the trapped-volume test on the implicit this way, with no mesh. The solid's own
    /// Lipschitz bound lets whole blocks of cells far from the surface be classified from one value.
    /// A solid whose bounds are not finite has no grid and throws `ImplicitMeshingError.empty`.
    /// - Throws: `ImplicitMeshingError.empty` and `CancellationError`.
    public static func of(_ solid: ImplicitSolid, using evaluator: any ImplicitBatchEvaluator) async throws -> OccupancyGrid {
        let box = solid.bounds
        guard [box.min.x, box.min.y, box.min.z, box.max.x, box.max.y, box.max.z].allSatisfy(\.isFinite) else {
            throw ImplicitMeshingError.empty
        }
        var grid = OccupancyGrid(bounds: box)
        try await grid.fill(lipschitz: solid.lipschitz, awaiting: { points in
            let values = try await evaluator.evaluate(points.map { SIMD3(Float($0.x), Float($0.y), Float($0.z)) })
            return values.map { Double($0) }
        })
        return grid
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Mesh/Checks/DrainHoles.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGeometry

/// Drain holes (implicit spec §3.6, §4.6): where the enclosed voids of an implicit solid are, and the solid with a round hole drilled
/// from each of them to the outside.
public enum DrainHoles {
    /// The lowest point of each enclosed void of `solid` along `direction` (the cell centre farthest in that direction, the middle one
    /// of those in the lowest layer), one per void, in the order of the voids. The voids are found on the coarse grid of the trapped-volume check, evaluated on the implicit itself.
    /// - Throws: `ImplicitMeshingError.empty` for a solid with no finite bounds, and `CancellationError`.
    public static func drainPoints(of solid: ImplicitSolid, direction: Vector3,
                                   using evaluator: any ImplicitBatchEvaluator) async throws -> [Vector3] {
        let grid = try await OccupancyGrid.of(solid, using: evaluator)
        let found = try EnclosedVoids(grid)
        return found.voids.map { cells in
            let centres = cells.map { grid.center(of: Int($0)) }
            // Cells in the lowest layer tie; the one nearest their middle is where the hole goes.
            let lowest = centres.map { $0.dot(direction) }.max() ?? 0
            let layer = centres.filter { $0.dot(direction) >= lowest - 1e-9 }
            let middle = layer.reduce(Vector3.zero, +) * (1.0 / Double(layer.count))
            return layer.min { ($0 - middle).length < ($1 - middle).length } ?? centres[0]
        }
    }

    /// `solid` with a cylinder of `diameter` taken out of it at each of `points`, running from the point along `direction` until it
    /// has left the solid's box. `solid` itself when there are no points or `direction` has no length.
    public static func drilled(_ solid: ImplicitSolid, at points: [Vector3], diameter: Double, direction: Vector3) -> ImplicitSolid {
        guard !points.isEmpty, let axis = direction.normalized else { return solid }
        let reach = solid.bounds.size.length + diameter
        let holes = points.map { point -> ImplicitSolid in
            let centre = point + axis * (reach / 2)
            return .moved(.cylinder(radius: diameter / 2, halfHeight: reach / 2), frame(at: centre, facing: axis))
        }
        return .subtract([solid] + holes, blend: .constant(0))
    }

    /// A frame at `origin` whose Z axis is `axis` (a unit vector).
    static func frame(at origin: Vector3, facing axis: Vector3) -> RigidMove {
        let helper: Vector3 = abs(axis.x) < 0.9 ? .unitX : .unitY
        let x = (helper - axis * helper.dot(axis)).normalized ?? .unitX
        return RigidMove(origin: origin, xAxis: x, yAxis: axis.cross(x), zAxis: axis)
    }
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorNodes/Implicit/DrainMode.swift`:

```swift
/// How Drain Holes chooses where to drill (implicit spec §3.6): at points the person gives, or at the lowest point of every enclosed void.
enum DrainMode: Int, CaseIterable {
    case atPoints
    case automatic

    static let options = ["At Points", "Automatic"]
}
```

Create `Sources/CreatorNodes/Implicit/DrainHolesNode.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel

/// Drills drain holes (implicit spec §3.6): the implicit solid minus a cylinder per hole. At Points drills from each of `points` along
/// `direction` until the hole leaves the part. Automatic finds the enclosed voids on a coarse grid (the trapped-volume test, run on the
/// implicit itself) and drills from the lowest point of each along `direction` to the outside.
public enum DrainHolesNode: NodeDefinition {
    public static let typeID = "creator.drainHoles"
    public static let displayName = "Drain Holes"
    public static let category = NodeCategory.implicit
    public static let inputs = [
        SocketSpec("implicit", .implicit),
        SocketSpec("diameter", .number, defaultValue: .number(2), unit: .millimetres, range: 0.1...50),
        SocketSpec("direction", .vector, defaultValue: .vector(Vector3(0, 0, -1))),
        SocketSpec("mode", .integer, defaultValue: .integer(DrainMode.automatic.rawValue)),
        SocketSpec("points", .vector, access: .list, optional: true),
    ]
    public static let outputs = [SocketSpec("implicit", .implicit)]
    public static let inspector = [
        InspectorSection(title: "Drain Holes", controls: [
            .segmented("mode", options: DrainMode.options), .number("diameter"), .vector("direction"),
            .note("Automatic drills from the lowest point of every enclosed void. At Points drills from the points wired in."),
        ]),
    ]

    public static func evaluate(_ inputs: NodeInputs, kernel: any Kernel, context: EvalContext) async throws -> NodeOutputs {
        let solid = try inputs.implicitSolid("implicit")
        let diameter = try inputs.number("diameter")
        guard diameter.isFinite, diameter >= 0.1 else { throw NodeError.invalidValue("“diameter” must be at least 0.1 mm.") }
        guard let direction = try inputs.vector("direction").normalized, direction.isFinite else {
            throw NodeError.invalidValue("“direction” can't be zero.")
        }
        let mode = DrainMode(rawValue: try inputs.choice("mode", options: DrainMode.options)) ?? .automatic
        var warnings: [String] = []
        let size = solid.bounds.size
        if size.x.isFinite, size.y.isFinite, size.z.isFinite {
            warnings += CheckWording.smallHole(diameter: diameter, seen: OccupancyGrid.smallestSeenHole(forSize: size))
        }
        var points: [Vector3]
        switch mode {
        case .atPoints:
            points = inputs.has("points") ? try inputs.vectors("points") : []
            guard !points.isEmpty else { throw NodeError.invalidValue("Connect points to “points”, or choose Automatic.") }
        case .automatic:
            let selection = await ImplicitEvaluators.make(for: solid, choice: context.implicitEngine)
            warnings += selection.warning.map { [$0] } ?? []
            do {
                points = try await DrainHoles.drainPoints(of: solid, direction: direction, using: selection.evaluator)
            } catch let error as ImplicitMeshingError {
                throw NodeError.invalidValue(error.userMessage)
            }
        }
        let result = DrainHoles.drilled(solid, at: points, diameter: diameter, direction: direction)
        return NodeOutputs(["implicit": .implicit(result.reference)], warnings: warnings)
    }
}
```

```swift
    /// The warning of a drain hole smaller than the check's grid can see through (`OccupancyGrid.smallestSeenHole`); empty when it is not.
    static func smallHole(diameter: Double, seen: Double) -> [String] {
        guard diameter < seen else { return [] }
        let (size, limit) = (diameter.millimetreText(locale: .messages), seen.millimetreText(locale: .messages))
        let text = "A hole of \(size) mm is smaller than Check Trapped Volumes can see through (\(limit) mm for this part), "
            + "so it may still report the void."
        return [text]
    }

    /// `text`, then the label in brackets when there is one, then the full stop.
```

### Task 6: Mesh to Solid (the OCCT shim `occt_mesh_to_solid`)

Spec §4.7: *builds a planar face per triangle, sews them (`BRepBuilderAPI_Sewing`), merges coplanar faces (`ShapeUpgrade_UnifySameDomain`), checks
`BRepCheck_Analyzer` and a closed shell, and makes the solid. A failure returns OCCT's reason as the node's error. Topology tags: every face gets
the node's operation tag.* §3.6: refused above 100,000 triangles with "Mesh has N triangles; Mesh to Solid takes at most 100,000. Decimate it first or
coarsen the resolution." macOS only, like the rest of OpenCascade until xp-2: the shim, its Swift wrapper and the OCCT tests are in macOS-only
targets; the **node, the protocol requirement and the cap are portable**.

- **The seam.** `Kernel` gains `meshToSolid(positions:triangles:tag:)` taking plain arrays (`CreatorKernel` cannot see `TriangleMesh`). A protocol
  extension gives every kernel a refusal, "Mesh to solid isn't supported by this kernel yet." (`FakeKernel` and the test kernels need no change);
  `KernelUnavailable` answers "Geometry isn't available on this platform yet." like every other operation.
- **The shim sews by construction, not by `BRepBuilderAPI_Sewing`** (a deviation from the spec's named class, with its reason; see User decisions). Each mesh
  vertex becomes one `TopoDS_Vertex` and each undirected edge one `TopoDS_Edge`, shared by the two triangles that use it (one traversal forward, one reversed), and
  a planar face is made on each triangle's wire. That *is* the sewn result, found by index instead of by searching for coincident edges. The first version
  of this task used `BRepBuilderAPI_Sewing` and `ImplicitBench` row i-6 measured it: for 100,000 faces, **13 of 26 seconds were the sewing** (its cutting and
  analysis options made no difference, and the faces took 4 s, the merge 2 s, the check 5 s). Counting edge uses is also what gives the messages: an edge
  with one use is "not closed", with more than two "not a manifold", and one run the same way by both its triangles "not consistently wound".
- **Shells and voids.** Triangles joined by edges are one surface, a shell with its signed volume. A surface with positive volume is an outer one (a solid each; several
  give a compound); one with negative volume faces into a cavity and is added to the smallest solid whose box holds it. (A mesh from the Mesh node with a cavity is
  two surfaces wound opposite ways, which is exactly OCCT's inner shell.) All surfaces negative is "wound inside out".
- **The merge runs only when it can do something.** `ShapeUpgrade_UnifySameDomain` joins faces in one plane to OCCT's own tolerances (1e-7 mm, 1e-12 rad). A mesh kept in
  single precision is rarely that flat (a UV sphere's planar quads are off by 1e-6 mm, so the 100,000-triangle benchmark mesh has nothing to merge), so the shim looks
  at the triangles that share an edge, and calls the merge only when two lie in one plane to 1e-9 mm. A cube's twelve triangles become six faces; a decimated
  mesh's big triangles stay triangles. The check (`BRepCheck_Analyzer`) is the one required by §4.7, run in parallel over the faces.
- **Fewer merged faces after Decimate is expected, and tested.** A decimated mesh's neighbouring planes differ by float noise, so the merge finds less to join than on an exact mesh: a
  decimated ball keeps every facet as a face (no two are coplanar) and a decimated plate with a hole came out as 46 triangles and 26 faces. The solid must still pass `BRepCheck` and have the
  mesh's volume to 1e-6 (`aDecimatedMeshBecomesAValidSolidOfTheMeshsVolumeWithFewerFacesThanTheMeshHadTriangles`, a ball and a bracket-like plate, through the real Decimate node).
- It refuses, in words: fewer than four triangles; a vertex that is not a number; an index past the vertices; a triangle with a repeated vertex, with no area, or whose
  edge is too short for OpenCascade; the three edge counts above; "wound inside out"; "OpenCascade rejected the solid".
- **OCCT's own reason is shown.** `KernelError.plainReason` hides OCCT's exception text behind "the geometry could not be built"; here the reason
  is the point, so `OCCTKernel.meshToSolid` passes it through (without the "occt: " prefix).
- **Welding first.** The snap can leave two vertices of a sliver 1e-8 apart, which OCCT cannot build a polygon from. `MeshWelder` merges vertices
  that round to the same 1e-6 mm cell and drops the triangles that collapse, which keeps a closed mesh closed.
- **Tags.** The result goes through `solid(from:history:[]...)`, so every face carries `TopoTag(node, item, .unnamed(face:))` and a face pick on
  it works by tag and normal like any solid's (`everyFaceCarriesTheOperationsTag`).
