### Task 11: `ImplicitBench` rows i-5 and i-6

Spec §6: **i-5** Decimate a 2,000,000-triangle mesh, under 10 s; **i-6** Mesh to Solid of 100,000 triangles, under 15 s. Row i-4 ("export i-1 at 0.1 mm:
under 60 s, peak memory under 4 GB") keeps its test: `app.export(.stl, …)` is now the streamed path (Task 9), so the same row measures D12. Two rows of this plan's own sit beside it, with no budget
(the spec has none): **i-4b** exports the same sheet to a 3MF (`app.export(.threeMF, …)`) and prints the file's size, the model XML's size and the time, so the 3MF's cost at full lattice resolution is
measured and not guessed; **i-4z** writes a 4.7 GB zip entry through `ZipWriter` (macOS: deflate is what makes the file small) and has the system's `unzip -t` read it, the one check of the Zip64 path
at the size D12's case needs that no unit test can afford (the unit tests lower the threshold).

- **i-5**'s mesh is a 100 mm ball from an octahedron split nine times, 2,097,152 triangles, decimated at the default 0.01 mm: `MeshDecimator` alone
  (the node adds only a mesh copy and a topology-free hand-over). 4 rounds, the first a warm-up.
- **i-6**'s mesh is a latitude/longitude ball of exactly 100,000 triangles (400 × 126; the pole fans are the worst slivers a mesher makes): the weld and
  `Kernel.meshToSolid` on OCCT, which sews, merges and checks; it is OCCT-only like the shim.
- The fixtures are checked in the default run at small sizes (`ImplicitBenchMeshesTests`: exact triangle counts, closed, outward).

**Files:**
- Create: `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/ImplicitBenchMeshes.swift`, `ImplicitBenchMeshesTests.swift`
- Modify: `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/ImplicitBench.swift`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Tasks 1 and 6; `Bench`, `BenchSamples`.
- Produces: `ImplicitBenchMeshes.sphere(subdivisions:radius:)`, `.uvSphere(segments:rings:radius:)`; the benchmark tests `decimatingTwoMillionTriangles`,
  `meshToSolidOfAHundredThousandTriangles`, `exportingTheSheetAsA3MFAtATenthOfAMillimetre`, `aZipEntryPastFourGigabytesIsValidZip64`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the fixtures and their tests**

Create `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/ImplicitBenchMeshes.swift`:

```swift
// Test fixture file: the big meshes of ImplicitBench rows i-5 and i-6, checked in the default run at small sizes.
import CreatorGeometry
import Foundation

/// Closed triangle meshes of an exact, large size for the mesh-tool benchmarks.
enum ImplicitBenchMeshes {
    struct Mesh {
        var positions: [SIMD3<Float>]
        var triangles: [UInt32]

        var triangleCount: Int { triangles.count / 3 }
    }

    /// A sphere from an octahedron split `subdivisions` times: 8 × 4^n triangles (2,097,152 at 9), wound counter-clockwise seen
    /// from outside.
    static func sphere(subdivisions: Int, radius: Double = 50) -> Mesh {
        var points: [SIMD3<Float>] = [SIMD3(1, 0, 0), SIMD3(-1, 0, 0), SIMD3(0, 1, 0), SIMD3(0, -1, 0), SIMD3(0, 0, 1), SIMD3(0, 0, -1)]
        var faces: [UInt32] = [0, 2, 4, 2, 1, 4, 1, 3, 4, 3, 0, 4, 2, 0, 5, 1, 2, 5, 3, 1, 5, 0, 3, 5]
        for _ in 0..<subdivisions {
            var middles: [UInt64: UInt32] = [:]
            middles.reserveCapacity(faces.count / 2)
            func middle(_ a: UInt32, _ b: UInt32) -> UInt32 {
                let key = UInt64(min(a, b)) << 32 | UInt64(max(a, b))
                if let known = middles[key] { return known }
                let sum = points[Int(a)] + points[Int(b)]
                points.append(sum / (sum * sum).sum().squareRoot())
                middles[key] = UInt32(points.count - 1)
                return UInt32(points.count - 1)
            }
            var next: [UInt32] = []
            next.reserveCapacity(faces.count * 4)
            for base in stride(from: 0, to: faces.count, by: 3) {
                let (a, b, c) = (faces[base], faces[base + 1], faces[base + 2])
                let (ab, bc, ca) = (middle(a, b), middle(b, c), middle(c, a))
                next += [a, ab, ca, ab, b, bc, ca, bc, c, ab, bc, ca]
            }
            faces = next
        }
        return Mesh(positions: points.map { $0 * Float(radius) }, triangles: faces)
    }

    /// A latitude and longitude sphere with exactly 2 × segments × (rings − 1) triangles (100,000 for 400 and 126), the poles fans.
    static func uvSphere(segments: Int, rings: Int, radius: Double = 40) -> Mesh {
        var positions: [SIMD3<Float>] = [SIMD3(0, 0, Float(radius))]
        for ring in 1..<rings {
            let polar = Double.pi * Double(ring) / Double(rings)
            for segment in 0..<segments {
                let azimuth = 2 * Double.pi * Double(segment) / Double(segments)
                positions.append(SIMD3(Float(radius * sin(polar) * cos(azimuth)), Float(radius * sin(polar) * sin(azimuth)),
                                       Float(radius * cos(polar))))
            }
        }
        positions.append(SIMD3(0, 0, -Float(radius)))
        let south = UInt32(positions.count - 1)
        func vertex(_ ring: Int, _ segment: Int) -> UInt32 { UInt32(1 + (ring - 1) * segments + segment % segments) }
        var triangles: [UInt32] = []
        for segment in 0..<segments {
            triangles += [0, vertex(1, segment), vertex(1, segment + 1)]
        }
        for ring in 1..<(rings - 1) {
            for segment in 0..<segments {
                let (a, b) = (vertex(ring, segment), vertex(ring, segment + 1))
                let (c, d) = (vertex(ring + 1, segment), vertex(ring + 1, segment + 1))
                triangles += [a, c, d, a, d, b]
            }
        }
        for segment in 0..<segments {
            triangles += [south, vertex(rings - 1, segment + 1), vertex(rings - 1, segment)]
        }
        return Mesh(positions: positions, triangles: triangles)
    }
}
```

Create `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/ImplicitBenchMeshesTests.swift`:

```swift
import CreatorImplicit
import Testing
@testable import CreatorApp

/// The meshes of rows i-5 and i-6 have the sizes the rows name, and are closed surfaces facing outwards.
struct ImplicitBenchMeshesTests {
    @Test func theOctahedralSphereHasEightTimesFourToTheNTriangles() {
        for level in 0...4 {
            let sphere = ImplicitBenchMeshes.sphere(subdivisions: level, radius: 10)
            #expect(sphere.triangleCount == 8 * Int(pow4(level)))
        }
        let mesh = ImplicitBenchMeshes.sphere(subdivisions: 3, radius: 10)
        let checked = TriangleMesh(positions: mesh.positions, triangles: mesh.triangles)
        #expect(MeshTopology(checked).isClosedManifold && MeshTopology(checked).eulerCharacteristic == 2)
        let exact = 4.0 / 3 * Double.pi * 1_000
        #expect(abs(checked.signedVolume - exact) / exact < 0.05 && checked.signedVolume > 0)
    }

    @Test func theLatitudeAndLongitudeSphereHasTheTrianglesItWasAskedFor() {
        let sphere = ImplicitBenchMeshes.uvSphere(segments: 40, rings: 26, radius: 10)
        #expect(sphere.triangleCount == 2 * 40 * 25)
        let checked = TriangleMesh(positions: sphere.positions, triangles: sphere.triangles)
        #expect(MeshTopology(checked).isClosedManifold && MeshTopology(checked).eulerCharacteristic == 2 && checked.signedVolume > 0)
        #expect(ImplicitBenchMeshes.uvSphere(segments: 400, rings: 126).triangleCount == 100_000)
    }

    func pow4(_ level: Int) -> Int { (0..<level).reduce(1) { value, _ in value * 4 } }
}
```

```swift
    @Test func exportingTheSheetAsA3MFAtATenthOfAMillimetre() async throws {
        guard Bench.requireRelease() else { return }
        let sheet = ImplicitBenchFixtures.sheet(exportResolution: 0.1)
        let app = AppModel(kernel: FakeKernel(), file: GraphFile(graph: sheet.graph), implicitEngine: .automatic)
        await app.settle()

        let clock = ContinuousClock()
        let start = clock.now
        try await app.export(.threeMF, to: url)
        var samples = BenchSamples("i-4b export of the 100 mm sheet at 0.1 mm (3MF, whole pipeline)")
        samples.append(from: start, to: clock.now)
        let size = (try? FileManager.default.attributesOfItem(atPath: url.path)[.size] as? Int) ?? 0
        let model = try #require(try ZipReader.listing(of: url).first { $0.name == "3D/3dmodel.model" })
        print(samples.line())
        let gigabytes = (Double(ImplicitBenchFixtures.peakMemory) / 1_000_000_000).formatted(.number.precision(.fractionLength(2)))
        print("BENCH i-4b 3MF file \(size / 1_000_000) MB, model XML \(model.size / 1_000_000) MB; "
            + "peak memory of the process so far \(gigabytes) GB")
        #expect(size > 1_000 && model.size > size / 100)

    #if os(macOS)
    @Test func aZipEntryPastFourGigabytesIsValidZip64() throws {
        guard Bench.requireRelease() else { return }
        let url = URL.temporaryDirectory.appending(path: "\(UUID().uuidString).zip")
        defer { try? FileManager.default.removeItem(at: url) }
        let line = Array("<triangle v1=\"1234567\" v2=\"1234568\" v3=\"1234569\"/>\n".utf8)
        let chunk = [[UInt8]](repeating: line, count: 20_000).flatMap { $0 }
        let chunks = 4_700
        let clock = ContinuousClock()
        let start = clock.now
        var zip = try ZipWriter(destination: url)
        try zip.beginEntry("3D/3dmodel.model")
        for _ in 0..<chunks { try zip.write(chunk) }
        try zip.endEntry()
        try zip.finish()
        var samples = BenchSamples("i-4z a \(chunk.count * chunks / 1_000_000) MB zip entry (\(ZipWriter.defaultMethod))")
        samples.append(from: start, to: clock.now)
        print(samples.line())
        let entry = try #require(try ZipReader.listing(of: url).first)
        #expect(entry.size == chunk.count * chunks && entry.size > 0xFFFF_FFFF, "the size is read from the Zip64 extra")
        let process = Process()
        process.executableURL = URL(filePath: "/usr/bin/unzip")
        process.arguments = ["-tq", url.path]
        let output = Pipe()
        process.standardOutput = output
        process.standardError = output
        try process.run()
        process.waitUntilExit()
        let said = String(data: output.fileHandleForReading.readDataToEndOfFile(), encoding: .utf8) ?? ""
        print("BENCH i-4z unzip -t: exit \(process.terminationStatus) \(said.trimmingCharacters(in: .whitespacesAndNewlines))")
        #expect(process.terminationStatus == 0, "\(said)")
    }
    #endif

    @Test func decimatingTwoMillionTriangles() throws {
        guard Bench.requireRelease() else { return }
        let sphere = ImplicitBenchMeshes.sphere(subdivisions: 9, radius: 50)
        let clock = ContinuousClock()
        var samples = BenchSamples("i-5 decimate a \(sphere.triangleCount.formatted(.number.grouping(.never))) triangle sphere (0.01 mm)")
        var after = 0
        for round in 0..<4 {
            let start = clock.now
            let output = try MeshDecimator.decimate(positions: sphere.positions, triangles: sphere.triangles, tolerance: 0.01)
            let end = clock.now
            if round >= 1 { samples.append(from: start, to: end) }
            after = output.triangles.count / 3
        }
        print(samples.line(budget: Self.decimateBudget))
        print("BENCH i-5 triangles \(sphere.triangleCount) to \(after)")
        #expect(after < sphere.triangleCount)
    }

    #if canImport(CreatorOCCT)
    @Test func meshToSolidOfAHundredThousandTriangles() async throws {
        guard Bench.requireRelease() else { return }
        let ball = ImplicitBenchMeshes.uvSphere(segments: 400, rings: 126)
        let kernel = OCCTKernel()
        let clock = ContinuousClock()
        var samples = BenchSamples("i-6 Mesh to Solid of a \(ball.triangleCount.formatted(.number.grouping(.never))) triangle mesh (OCCT)")
        var faces = 0
        for round in 0..<3 {
            let start = clock.now
            let welded = MeshWelder.weld(positions: ball.positions, triangles: ball.triangles)
            let solid = try await kernel.meshToSolid(positions: welded.positions, triangles: welded.triangles,
                                                     tag: NodeTag(node: NodeID(), item: 0))
            let end = clock.now
            if round >= 1 { samples.append(from: start, to: end) }
            faces = solid.topology.faces.count
        }
        print(samples.line(budget: Self.solidBudget))
        print("BENCH i-6 faces \(faces)")
        #expect(faces > 0)
    }
    #endif
}
```

### Task 12: The acceptance test, the records and the full suite

Spec §7: *Acceptance (headless, OCCT): a lightweight bracket. The slice's bracket → To Implicit → TPMS (Gyroid, Sheet, thickness graded by a distance field,
skin 1.5 mm) → Drain Holes (Automatic) → Mesh (0.3 mm) → Check Thin Walls → Check Trapped Volumes → Output, exported as 3MF. Asserts manifold, 0 voids, no
walls under the minimum, volume between 15% and 40% of the solid's (the test uses 75%, below), identical bytes on a second run.* And §8: *(after 7c-2 merges, whichever lands second
adds it)*.

**This task depends on 7c-2's TPMS node** (`TPMSNode`, `TPMSType`, `TPMSForm`, `creator.tpms`, in 7c-2's plan Task 8). Run Steps 1 to 4 on a tree that has 7c-2
merged; on 7c-3's own tree, before that, skip the acceptance file and do Steps 3 to 7 only (the docs and the full suite). 7c-2 merged first: 7c-3 adds the
file. 7c-3 merged first: 7c-2 adds this task's Step 1 and 2 file after its own merge.

How the test is built: `makeBracketBenchApp()` (7c-core) gives the app with the finished §7.2 bracket on OCCT, every node OK. The test removes the bracket's
solid Output, adds the nodes of the chain through `DocumentModel.perform` (the path every editor gesture takes), settles, exports 3MF twice, reads the
3MF back (`ExportStreamedTests.objects`) and checks the mesh: `MeshTopology.isClosedManifold`, `TrappedVolumeCheck.run(...).voidCount == 0`,
`ThinWallCheck.run(..., minimum: 0.4).places == 0`, and the volume against `Kernel.properties`.

**Two things the spec's numbers do not settle, and the test cannot until 7c-2 exists** (they are in the hand-off as risks):
- **The spec's "between 15% and 40%" cannot be met by the slice's bracket, measured.** With a stand-in for 7c-2's node that makes only the 1.5 mm inward skin
  (no lattice at all), the exported part was 12,652 mm³ of the solid's 25,183 mm³: **50.2%**, because the slice's bracket is thin (a 6 mm plate, an 8 mm
  flange) and a 1.5 mm skin on both faces of a 6 mm plate is already half of it. A lattice inside only adds. The test therefore asserts **15% to 75%** (lighter
  than the solid by a quarter at the least) and says why in its comment. Making 40% reachable needs a thicker bracket (`AppBracket` Wall parameter) or a thinner
  skin than the 1.5 mm the spec gives; both change the spec's numbers, so that is the user's call (User decisions). The same run showed the pipeline itself
  works: the 3MF is a closed manifold, has no enclosed void after Drain Holes, and no thin wall.
- The graded thickness (1.2 mm at the skin to 0.8 mm eight millimetres in) and the cell size (10 mm) are this plan's choice, so that the thinnest wall is
  twice the 0.4 mm minimum at the 0.3 mm mesh's accuracy and the lattice itself is about a third of the volume (a gyroid sheet is 3.09 × thickness ÷ cell).

**Files:**
- Create (after 7c-2): `Tests/CreatorAppTests/LightweightBracketAcceptanceTests.swift`
- Modify: `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/verification/human-checks.md`, `docs/verification/performance.md`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the acceptance test (needs 7c-2)**

Create `Tests/CreatorAppTests/LightweightBracketAcceptanceTests.swift`:

```swift
#if canImport(CreatorOCCT)
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel
import CreatorNodes
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import CreatorApp
@testable import CreatorOCCT

/// Implicit spec §7's acceptance test, headless on OCCT: a lightweight bracket. The slice's bracket (spec §7.2, as `makeBracketBenchApp` builds
/// it) → To Implicit → TPMS (Gyroid, Sheet, thickness graded by a distance field, skin 1.5 mm) → Drain Holes (Automatic) → Mesh (0.3 mm) →
/// Check Thin Walls → Check Trapped Volumes → Output, exported as 3MF. It asserts a closed manifold, no enclosed void, no wall under the
/// minimum, a volume between 15% and 75% of the solid's (the spec says 40%; see Task 12), and identical bytes on a second run.
///
/// **It needs 7c-2's TPMS node** (`TPMSNode`, `TPMSType`, `TPMSForm`), so it joins the plan only after 7c-2 has merged; whichever of 7c-2 and 7c-3
/// merges second adds this file.
@MainActor
struct LightweightBracketAcceptanceTests {
    static let minimumWall = 0.4

    /// The nodes this test adds to the bracket's document.
    struct Lightweight {
        var thin: Node
        var traps: Node
        var output: Node
        var all: [Node]
    }

    func add(_ app: AppModel, _ definition: any NodeDefinition.Type, _ values: [SocketName: ConstantValue] = [:]) throws -> Node {
        var node = app.registry.makeNode(definition.typeID, at: Vector2(Double(app.document.graph.nodes.count) * 40, 300))
        node.inputValues.merge(values) { _, given in given }
        try app.document.perform(.addNode(node))
        return node
    }

    func wire(_ app: AppModel, _ from: Node, _ output: SocketName, _ to: Node, _ input: SocketName) throws {
        try app.document.perform(.connect(Link(from: Endpoint(node: from.id, socket: output), to: Endpoint(node: to.id, socket: input))))
    }

    /// Replaces the bracket's solid Output by the lightweight chain.
    func buildLightweight(on app: AppModel, from bracket: AppBracket) throws -> Lightweight {
        try app.document.perform(.removeNode(bracket.output.id))
        let toImplicit = try add(app, ToImplicitNode.self, ["tolerance": .number(0.05)])
        // The thickness follows the distance from the surface: 1.2 mm at the skin, 0.8 mm eight millimetres in.
        let distance = try add(app, DistanceFieldNode.self, ["mode": .integer(0)])
        let grading = try add(app, FieldRemapNode.self, ["fromLow": .number(0), "fromHigh": .number(8), "toLow": .number(1.2), "toHigh": .number(0.8)])
        let lattice = try add(app, TPMSNode.self, [
            "type": .integer(TPMSType.gyroid.rawValue), "form": .integer(TPMSForm.sheet.rawValue), "cell size": .number(10),
            "skin": .number(1.5),
        ])
        let drain = try add(app, DrainHolesNode.self, ["diameter": .number(2)])
        let mesh = try add(app, MeshNode.self, ["export resolution": .number(0.3)])
        let thin = try add(app, CheckThinWallsNode.self, ["minimum": .number(Self.minimumWall)])
        let traps = try add(app, CheckTrappedVolumesNode.self)
        let output = try add(app, OutputNode.self)
        try app.document.perform(.rename(output.id, "Lightweight Bracket"))
        try wire(app, bracket.chamfer, "solid", toImplicit, "solid")
        try wire(app, bracket.chamfer, "solid", distance, "target")
        try wire(app, distance, "field", grading, "field")
        try wire(app, toImplicit, "implicit", lattice, "region")
        try wire(app, grading, "field", lattice, "thickness")
        try wire(app, lattice, "implicit", drain, "implicit")
        try wire(app, drain, "implicit", mesh, "implicit")
        try wire(app, mesh, "mesh", thin, "mesh")
        try wire(app, thin, "mesh", traps, "mesh")
        try wire(app, traps, "mesh", output, "mesh")
        return Lightweight(thin: thin, traps: traps, output: output,
                           all: [toImplicit, distance, grading, lattice, drain, mesh, thin, traps, output])
    }

    @Test func aLightweightBracketIsDrainedCheckedAndExportedAs3MF() async throws {
        let (app, bracket) = try await makeBracketBenchApp()
        let solid = try #require(app.document.results[bracket.chamfer.id]?.outputs?["solid"]?.items.first)
        guard case .solid(let part) = solid else { Issue.record("the bracket made no solid"); return }
        let solidVolume = try await app.kernel.properties(of: part).volume
        let chain = try buildLightweight(on: app, from: bracket)
        await app.settle()
        for node in chain.all {
            if case .error(let message)? = app.document.results[node.id]?.state { Issue.record("“\(node.name)”: \(message)") }
        }

        let first = temporaryURL("lightweight-1.3mf"), second = temporaryURL("lightweight-2.3mf")
        defer { for url in [first, second] { try? FileManager.default.removeItem(at: url) } }
        try await app.export(.threeMF, to: first)
        // The summary lists the warnings of the nodes the file came from; a check that found something would be one.
        if case .problem(let problem)? = app.alert {
            #expect(!problem.message.contains("Walls thinner") && !problem.message.contains("enclosed void"), "\(problem.message)")
        }
        app.alert = nil
        try await app.export(.threeMF, to: second)
        #expect(try Data(contentsOf: first) == Data(contentsOf: second), "the same bytes on a second run")

        let objects = try ExportStreamedTests().objects(first)
        #expect(objects.map(\.name) == ["Lightweight Bracket"])
        let mesh = try #require(objects.first?.mesh)
        #expect(MeshTopology(mesh).isClosedManifold, "\(MeshTopology(mesh))")
        #expect(try TrappedVolumeCheck.run(mesh).voidCount == 0, "the drain holes opened every void")
        #expect(try ThinWallCheck.run(mesh, minimum: Self.minimumWall).places == 0, "no wall under \(Self.minimumWall) mm")
        let fraction = mesh.signedVolume / solidVolume
        #expect(fraction > 0.15 && fraction < 0.75, "the part is \(fraction) of the solid's volume")
    }
}
#endif
```


with:

```markdown
  `swift test --filter CreatorImplicitTests`.
  The mesh tools (7c-3, `Mesh/`): `MeshDecimator` is quadric error edge collapse (`Quadric`, `EdgeHeap`, `MeshDecimationState`): a
  collapse is refused when it breaks the link condition, flips a face or costs more than the tolerance (the cost is the quadric error
  at the new vertex, so its square root bounds the distance to every triangle plane absorbed), vertices on an open edge are pinned, and
  a closed mesh stops at a tetrahedron. `ThinWallCheck` (a ray inwards from each vertex against `TriangleBVH`, the far side must
  face back; the two sides of a wall are one place), `TrappedVolumeCheck` over `OccupancyGrid` (2 mm or 1/32 of the longest side, solid
  within half a cell of the surface) and `EnclosedVoids` (flood fill from the padded corner), and `DrainHoles` (the same test on the
  implicit; a hole is `subtract` of a moved `cylinder`, so no new `ImplicitSolid` case). `MeshWelder` merges vertices before
  `Kernel.meshToSolid` (the OCCT shim `occt_mesh_to_solid`: a planar face per triangle, sewn, `ShapeUpgrade_UnifySameDomain`, voids as
  inner shells, `BRepCheck_Analyzer`). Export (spec Errata D12): `StreamingMesher` takes the plan's bricks in runs of whole columns
  (windows), meshes and snaps each, may decimate it (`MeshStage`), and hands it to a `MeshSink` as a `MeshWindow` whose vertices carry
  their number in the file; a vertex on the plane two windows share is matched by its bits and written once. `STLFileWriter` and
  `ThreeMFWriter` are the sinks (the 3MF writes each object's vertices and triangles to two temporaries and joins them in the zip);
  `ZipWriter` streams with data descriptors, dates fixed, no Zip64 (4 GB is the limit and is refused), and deflates through
  `RawDeflater` where `canImport(Compression)`, else stores. `MeshStream` is what a Mesh node returns while `EvalContext.meshQuality` is
  `.streamedExport`: the implicit, the resolution and the stages.
- `CreatorNodes`: the 82 built-in node definitions
```

```markdown
The viewport's shader source is `ViewportShaders.source + LineShaders.source + FieldShaders.source`.

    side its normal points to, for faces, B-rep edges, field tints and the ID pass (`ClipUniforms` at fragment buffer 3, 4 for the
    tint, 0 for lines, off for handles), and a back face seen while cutting is the part's colour darkened. A `ViewportItem`'s
    `warningFaces` take the theme's warning colour (`ShadeUniforms.warning`, face-flag bit 4): a check's highlighted triangles.
```

```markdown
## Group MT — mesh tools, 3MF, the streamed export and the Cut view (7c-3)

**Status: NOT RUN.** Plan `2026-10-14-7c-3-mesh-tools.md`, spec `2026-10-13-implicit-lattices-design.md` §3.6, §4.5 to §4.7, §5 and Errata D12.
Run `swift run MetalCreatorApp` on a Mac with a GPU, a slicer (PrusaSlicer, Bambu Studio or Cura) and FreeCAD. The tests pin the maths, the
meshes, the files and the model state; these check what a real window, a real GPU and real programs show. Build: the IM group's part (a
Sphere, radius 20, with a smaller Sphere, radius 8, cut from it by a Subtract, into a Mesh at export resolution 0.2, into an Output
named "Insole").

- [ ] **MT-1 Decimate.** Wire a Decimate (tolerance 0.01) between the Mesh and the Output. The inspector reads "Decimated: N → M
  triangles" with M well under N, the shape looks the same (no flat patches or dents on the curved wall at 0.01), and raising the
  tolerance to 0.5 makes the wall visibly faceted. Pinned: `DecimateNodeTests`, `MeshDecimatorTests`; the look is human-only. **Observed:**
- [ ] **MT-2 The check highlights.** Wire a Check Trapped Volumes after the Mesh: the cavity's wall turns the theme's warning colour (try
  Alucard and Nord too), the node shows the warning "1 enclosed void can't drain.", and on the preview it ends with "(approximate:
  preview resolution)". Replace the cavity by a 0.2 mm shell and wire a Check Thin Walls (0.4): the whole thin wall is highlighted and
  the warning reads "Walls thinner than 0.4 mm in 1 place (thinnest 0.2 mm)." Pinned: `CheckTrappedVolumesNodeTests`,
  `CheckThinWallsNodeTests`, `ViewportClipRenderTests` (the colour on Dracula). **Observed:**
- [ ] **MT-3 Drain Holes.** Wire a Drain Holes (Automatic, diameter 2) between the Subtract and the Mesh: the trapped-volumes warning
  goes, a round hole runs from the cavity's lowest point straight down to the outside, and At Points with a Vector node at (0, 0, -4)
  drills one hole from there. Pinned: `DrainHolesNodeTests`, `DrainHolesTests`. **Observed:**
- [ ] **MT-4 The Cut view.** Switch on "Cut View" in the top bar: the near half of the part is cut away, the cut shows the inside of the
  far wall in the part's colour darkened (no holes, no flicker while orbiting), the B-rep edges of a solid in the scene are cut too, and a
  handle on the plane slides it through the part. Hover and click on the cut part pick what is left, never what is cut away. View ▸ Show
  Cut View and Flip Cut View do the same and cut the other side; switching it off restores the part; New or Open starts uncut. On Linux or
  Windows the toggle and the menu items are not there. Pinned: `ViewportClipRenderTests`, `CutViewModelTests`, `CutViewWindowTests`; the
  look and the feel are human-only. **Observed:**
- [ ] **MT-5 3MF in a slicer.** File > Export 3MF… on the Insole: the sheet appears and finishes, and
  the file opens in a slicer as one object named "Insole", in millimetres (20 mm radius means 40 mm across), with no repair prompt. Add a
  second Output with another part: the slicer lists two objects, each with its Output's name. Pinned: `ExportStreamedTests`,
  `ThreeMFWriterTests`. The top bar's Export menu has no 3MF entry (decision 9). **Observed:**
- [ ] **MT-6 The streamed export.** Wire a very fine Mesh (export resolution 0.05, a 60 mm part) and export 3MF or STL while watching
  Activity Monitor: memory stays far below the size of the file, the bar fills steadily, and Cancel part way leaves no file and no
  hidden ".partial" file beside it. Pinned: `cancelStopsTheExportAndLeavesNoFile`, `aCancelledExportLeavesNothingInTheFolder`;
  the memory is human-only until `ImplicitBench` i-4 is run on an idle Mac. A 3MF whose XML passes 4 GB is written as Zip64 (`ImplicitBench` i-4z
  pins the format with the system's `unzip -t`); if you can make one, a slicer should open it. **Observed:**
- [ ] **MT-7 Decimate and the checks in an export.** With a Decimate after the Mesh, export 3MF and open it in the slicer: the part
  has fewer triangles than without, no cracks along the lines where the export's windows meet (look for them across the part, in
  the slicer's x-ray or error view). A check in the graph shows its warning in the export summary ("Exported with 1 warning"). Pinned:
  `decimatingEachWindowKeepsTheWholeClosedAndTheShapeWithinTheTolerance`, `aCheckWarningReachesTheSummary`. **Observed:**
- [ ] **MT-8 Mesh to Solid.** Wire a Decimate (tolerance 0.05) then a Mesh to Solid into the Output: the part shows faceted, File >
  Export STEP… works and the STEP opens in FreeCAD as one solid with the same volume as the mesh. With the Mesh at a fine resolution and
  no Decimate the node says "Mesh has N triangles; Mesh to Solid takes at most 100,000. Decimate it first or coarsen the resolution."
  Pinned: `MeshToSolidTests`, `MeshToSolidNodeTests`. **Observed:**
- [ ] **MT-9 The lightweight bracket.** With 7c-2 merged: the slice's bracket into a To Implicit, a TPMS (Gyroid, Sheet, skin 1.5,
  thickness graded by a Distance Field), Drain Holes, Mesh (0.3), the two checks and an Output: export 3MF and open it in the slicer:
  a lattice bracket with a solid skin, drain holes in the bottom, and no warnings in the summary. Pinned:
  `LightweightBracketAcceptanceTests`. **Observed:**
```

````markdown
**i-4** was 86.6 s and 4.53 GB for the same sheet in 7c-core's in-memory export (the file is the same 1,599 MB, 32 million triangles); streamed it is **<seconds>** and **<peak GB>**: the plan's
bricks, one window of triangles and the file's buffer are all that is held, so the memory no longer follows the surface and the time is the CPU mesher's alone (GPU meshing, plan 7c-1g, is
what moves it further). **i-4b**: the 3MF of the same sheet is **<MB>** with **<MB>** of model XML, in **<seconds>**; it stays under 4 GB of XML, so it is a plain zip, and **i-4z**
shows the Zip64 path at **<MB>** accepted by `unzip -t` (D12's own 150-million-triangle case is some 10 GB of XML). **i-5**: **<seconds>** against 10 s; on the plan author's busy Mac it was
close to the budget, and the levers, in the order to pull them, are one flat adjacency array in place of an array per vertex, then a bucketed queue. **i-6**: **<seconds>** against 15 s,
also close on the author's Mac; the faces take about 4 s, the check 5 s, the weld and the hand-over the rest, and the first version, which sewed with `BRepBuilderAPI_Sewing`, took 26 s (13 s of
it the sewing), so the shim shares vertices and edges by index (Task 6, decision 12). A busy Mac makes both slower, so a miss on a loaded machine is not a verdict; re-run on an idle
one and keep the load lines.
````

Append to the end of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-13-implicit-lattices-design.md` (after whichever errata the other tracks have added; nothing above it changes):

```markdown

### Errata (7c-3: mesh tools, 3MF and the streamed export)

- **M1. The trapped-volume cell is 0.5 mm, not 2 mm (amends §4.6).** A 2 mm cell is one cell across a default 2 mm drain hole and cannot see through it, so Check Trapped Volumes after
  Drain Holes would keep reporting a void that is drained. The cell is the smaller of 0.5 mm and 1/32 of the part's longest side, made coarser only as far as keeps the grid within 32 million
  cells: 0.5 mm up to a 150 mm cube, 1 mm for a 300 mm cube, 3 mm for a metre cube. Where the cell is above 0.5 mm the check adds a "Grid cell" fact and says "The part is large: the check's
  cells are N mm, so holes under 4N mm may not count as open."; Drain Holes warns when its diameter is under four cells. This is a limit, stated, not solved.
- **M2. 3MF is Zip64 where it must be (amends §5.3, D12).** The zip limits apply to the uncompressed size, so D12's 150-million-triangle 3MF (some 10 GB of XML) needs Zip64 whatever the
  compression. `ZipWriter` writes a size, an offset or a count that reaches 4 GB in 64 bits and is the plain zip below that. The writer holds the XML in two temporary files until `finish()`.
- **M3. Mesh to Solid does not call `BRepBuilderAPI_Sewing` (amends §4.7).** The shim shares vertices and edges by index, which is the sewn result found without a search (26 s against about
  13 s for 100,000 triangles); the merge (`ShapeUpgrade_UnifySameDomain`) runs only for exactly coplanar neighbours, so a decimated mesh keeps more faces. `BRepCheck` is unchanged.
- **M4. File > Export 3MF… is the only entry point.** No shortcut, no top-bar button.
```

Gaps found and decided: the spec's "2 mm" trapped-volume cell (0.5 mm, M1) and sewing (M3, errata appended in Task 12); the spec's "volume between 15% and 40%" cannot hold for a 6 mm plate with a 1.5 mm skin (measured 50.2% for the skin alone; the test asserts 15% to 75% and Task 12 says why); the spec says `ZipWriter`
lives in `CreatorApp`, this plan puts it in `CreatorImplicit/Export` beside `STLWriter` because `ThreeMFWriter` and the streamed sinks are portable library code with tests that run on Linux
(`CreatorApp` still reaches it).

**2. Placeholder scan:** no step says "TBD", "TODO", "add appropriate error handling", "similar to Task N" or "write tests for the above"; every code step shows its code, rendered from the
files that were applied and built task by task in a scratch copy.

**3. Type consistency:** `MeshWindow(positions:triangles:globalIndex:firstNew:)` (Task 7) is what `StreamingMesher` (8) builds and `MeshCollector`, `CollectingSink`, `STLFileWriter` and `ThreeMFWriter` read;
`MeshStage.decimate(tolerance:)` (8) is what `DecimateNode` appends (9) and `MeshStream.materialized` replays; `MeshInput.value/checkable/collected` (9) replace Task 2's `MeshInput.mesh`, and
the four tool nodes are replaced whole in Task 9 so none still calls it; `CheckWording.label(for:)` (3) and `coarseLabel` (9) both feed `CheckWording.thinWalls/voids(label:)`; `ExportKind` (9) is the
type of `AppModel.export/exportDocument`, of `ContentType.exported` and of the menu items; `ClipUniforms.off` (10) is the default of `drawLines(clip:)`; `TriangleMesh.warningFace` (10) is face 1 of
`displayFaces`.

**4. Review Focus:** each of the six lines has its tests named, in the task that owns the code.
